family of entries stays uninformative for exponentially many rounds while the
useful entries do not, and what it demonstrates is the method:
\Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier} can certify exponential lower bounds on
specified entries of $\Delta_k$. Whether the hybrid decides every stopping SSG
within polynomially many rounds remains open, and by
\Cref{thm:decide-one-bit} an affirmative answer would put \textsc{SSG-Value} in
$\mathsf P$. A refutation must drive $\Delta_k(v^{(i)},v)$ to $0$ for every
controlled $v$ and both $i$, which by \Cref{rem:own-successor} is a strictly
harder demand than the one $CC$ meets.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{A hybrid stall inside the few-denominator class}

The question left open after \Cref{prop:fv-stall} --- whether the
few-denominator class contains a stall at a value-distinguishing vertex
--- is answered here, affirmatively and at \emph{constant} denominator,
by the round-$16$ few-denominator route: a one-player family on which
the Z-seeded own-successor hybrid is silent for $2^{\Omega(N)}$ rounds,
proved by an explicit chain of convex certificates
(\Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier}); the multiplicative calculus of
\Cref{def:ratio}, which the certificate does not cover, is not stalled.
The wedge of \Cref{sec:wedge} already
stalls the hybrid at a value-distinguishing vertex; what is new is that
the deciding gap is $1/D$ with $D$ constant.

\begin{definition}[The coarse core]\label{def:cv}
For $e\ge1$ and $s\ge3$ let $\mathrm{CV}(e,s)$ have
$\Vmax=\{v_0,v_1,v_2\}$, $\Vmin=\emptyset$, every other non-sink average, and
\[
 \begin{aligned}
  &H\to(t_1,t_0);\\
  &A^{i}_q\to(v_{3-i},A^{i}_{q+1})\ (q<e),\qquad A^{i}_{e}\to(v_{3-i},H);\\
  &B^{i}_q\to(v_{i},B^{i}_{q+1})\ (q<2e),\qquad B^{i}_{2e}\to(v_{i},H);\\
  &v_i\to(A^{i}_1,B^{i}_1)\qquad(i=1,2);\\
  &d_q\to(v_1,d_{q+1})\ (q<s-1),\qquad d_{s-1}\to(v_1,t_0);\qquad
   v_0\to(H,d_1),
 \end{aligned}
\]
with start vertex $v_0$. Put $u:=2^{-e}$, $\lambda:=1-u$, $\beta:=1-u^{2}$,
$\gamma:=2^{-s}$, $\beta_0:=1-2\gamma$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Basic data]\label{prop:cv-values}
$\mathrm{CV}(e,s)$ is a stopping one-player SSG with $N=6e+s+5$ and
$a=N-5$; every non-sink has value $\tfrac12$ except
$w^\ast(d_q)=\tfrac12\bigl(1-2^{-(s-q)}\bigr)$, so
\[
  \Lambda(G)=\{0,1\}\cup\{\tfrac12\}\cup
  \{\tfrac12(1-2^{-r}):1\le r\le s-1\},\qquad k(G)=s+2,\qquad D(G)=2^{s},
\]
and $Z_0=\{t_0\}$, $Z_1=\{t_1\}$, so the free seed of \Cref{def:hybrid} adds
nothing. The vertex $v_0$ is value-distinguishing with gap exactly
$w^\ast(H)-w^\ast(d_1)=2^{-s}=1/D(G)$, the least a value-distinguishing vertex
can have; $v_1$ and $v_2$ are not value-distinguishing. Every parameter is read
on the subgame reachable from $v_0$, which is all of $G$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The displayed vector is a fixed point of $T$: at $H$ it reads
$\tfrac12(1+0)$; at $A^{i}_q$ and $B^{i}_q$ it is a mean of two vertices of
value $\tfrac12$; at $v_1,v_2$ it is $\max(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$; at $d_{s-1}$ it
reads $\tfrac12(\tfrac12+0)=\tfrac14$ and backwards
$w^\ast(d_q)=\tfrac12(\tfrac12+w^\ast(d_{q+1}))$ gives the display; at $v_0$ it
reads $\max(\tfrac12,\tfrac12-2^{-s})$. Stopping: let $U$ be a trap
(\Cref{lem:trapchar}). $H$ has the successor $t_0$, so $H\notin U$; then
$A^{i}_e,B^{i}_{2e}\notin U$ and inductively no $A^{i}_q$ or $B^{i}_q$ lies in
$U$, so $v_1,v_2\notin U$; $d_{s-1}$ has the successor $t_0$, so no $d_q$ lies
in $U$, and $v_0$ has no successor in $U$. Hence $U=\emptyset$, and the fixed
point is $w^\ast$ (\Cref{cor:comparison}). The denominator of
$w^\ast(d_1)=(2^{\,s-1}-1)/2^{s}$ is $2^{s}$ because $2^{\,s-1}-1$ is odd, and
no value has a larger one, so $D(G)=2^{s}$, attaining
\Cref{thm:alphabet-denominator}'s bound $2^{k-2}$. Counting:
$3+1+2(e+2e)+(s-1)=6e+s+3$ non-sinks.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The lift]\label{lem:cv-lift}
$\theta_{v_j}=e_j$, $\theta_H=\theta_{t_0}=\theta_{t_1}=0$, and
\[
  \theta_{A^{1}_q}=(1-2^{-(e-q+1)})e_2,\quad
  \theta_{A^{2}_q}=(1-2^{-(e-q+1)})e_1,\quad
  \theta_{B^{i}_q}=(1-2^{-(2e-q+1)})e_{i},\quad
  \theta_{d_q}=(1-2^{-(s-q)})e_1 .
\]
In particular $\theta_{A^{1}_1}=\lambda e_2$, $\theta_{B^{1}_1}=\beta e_1$,
$\theta_{A^{2}_1}=\lambda e_1$, $\theta_{B^{2}_1}=\beta e_2$,
$\theta_{d_1}=\beta_0e_1$; \emph{every} $\theta_x$ is $0$, $e_0$, or $ce_i$
with $i\in\{1,2\}$ and $c\in(0,1]$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Backward substitution: $p\to(b,p')$ has
$\theta_p=\tfrac12(\theta_b+\theta_{p'})$ and the last vertex of a chain has
$\theta=\tfrac12\theta_b$. Every chain reads exactly one of $v_1,v_2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The hybrid is silent for $2^{\Omega(N)}$ rounds]\label{thm:cv-lower}
Put $\ell:=\gamma/\beta_0$, $M_k:=\lambda^{k}/2$, and
\[
  \Xi_k:=\bigl\{\xi\in\mathbb R^{3}:\ \ell\le\xi_1\le M_k,\ \ell\le\xi_2\le M_k,\
  \xi_1\ge\lambda\xi_2,\ \xi_2\ge\lambda\xi_1,\ \xi_0=\beta_0\xi_1-\gamma\bigr\},
\]
$P_k:=\{x\mapsto w^\ast(x)+\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle:\xi\in\Xi_k\}$, and let
$K$ be the largest $k$ with $\lambda M_k\ge\ell$. Then $(P_k)_{k\le K}$
satisfies the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier}, so
$\Delta_k\ge\Lambda(P_k)$ for $k\le K$, and
\[
  \Delta_k(d_1,v_0)\;\ge\;0,\qquad \Delta_k(v_0,H)\;\ge\;\beta_0M_k-\gamma>0 ,
\]
while $\Delta_k(v_0,d_1)\ge\gamma>0$ and $\Delta_k(H,v_0)\ge0$ by
\Cref{thm:hybrid-sound}. Hence \emph{neither} clause of
\Cref{rem:own-successor} fires at $v_0$ at any round $k\le K$, and
\[
  K+1\;=\;\Bigl\lfloor\frac{\ln\bigl(\beta_0/2\gamma\bigr)}{\ln(1/\lambda)}
  \Bigr\rfloor
  \;\ge\;\Bigl\lfloor(2^{e}-1)\bigl[(s-1)\ln2+\ln(1-2^{1-s})\bigr]\Bigr\rfloor .
\]
With $s$ fixed and $e=(N-s-5)/6$ this is $2^{\Omega(N)}$ rounds at
$D(G)=2^{s}$ fixed. The certificate blocks $v_1$ and $v_2$ as well:
$\Lambda(P_k)(v_1,A^{1}_1)=(1-\lambda^{2})M_k>0$ and
$\Lambda(P_k)(v_1,B^{1}_1)=u^{2}M_k>0$, symmetrically at $v_2$, while the
reverse entries are $\ge0$ by \Cref{thm:hybrid-sound} because all three
values are $\tfrac12$; so for $k\le K$ the hybrid decides \emph{no}
controlled vertex.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $h_k(c):=\max_{\xi\in\Xi_k}\langle c,\xi\rangle$, so
$\Lambda(P_k)(x,y)=w^\ast(x)-w^\ast(y)+h_k(\theta_x-\theta_y)$. Note
$\Xi_{k+1}\subseteq\Xi_k$, hence $h_{k+1}\le h_k$ and
$\Lambda(P_{k+1})\le\Lambda(P_k)$; $\Xi_k\ne\emptyset$ because
$(\xi_1,\xi_2)=(M_k,M_k)$ is admissible, and $\ell\le\lambda M_k$ makes
$(M_k,\lambda M_k)$ and $(\lambda M_k,M_k)$ admissible too. On $\Xi_k$,
$\xi_1,\xi_2\ge\ell>0$ and $\xi_0=\beta_0\xi_1-\gamma\ge0$, so $\xi\ge0$ and
$0\le\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle\le M_k\le\tfrac12$ for every $x$.

\emph{(a) $P_k\subseteq Q(G)$.} Average rows: \Cref{lem:cv-lift}. Box rows:
$0\le w^\ast(x)\le\tfrac12$ and $0\le\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle\le\tfrac12$.
Max rows: at $v_1$, $\xi_1\ge\lambda\xi_2$ and $\xi_1\ge\beta\xi_1$; at $v_2$
symmetrically; at $v_0$, $\xi_0\ge0$ and $\xi_0\ge\beta_0\xi_1-\gamma$, the
last with equality.

\emph{(b) Average clauses.} If $x$ is average then $\theta_x$ and $w^\ast(x)$
are the means of those of its successors, so by sublinearity of $h_{k+1}$,
$\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,y)\le\tfrac12\sum_i\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x^{(i)},y)
\le\tfrac12\sum_i\Lambda(P_k)(x^{(i)},y)$; the $(\mathrm{down})$ clause at an
average $y$ and both average--average composites are the same computation,
using $\theta_x-\theta_y=\tfrac12\sum_i(\theta_{x^{(i)}}-\theta_{y^{(\pi(i))}})$.
There are no Min vertices.

\emph{(c) Base clauses.} $\Lambda(P_{k+1})\le1$;
$\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,x)=0$;
$\Lambda(P_{k+1})(t_0,y)=-w^\ast(y)-\min\langle\theta_y,\xi\rangle\le0$ as
$\theta_y\ge0$, $\xi\ge0$; $\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,t_1)\le\tfrac12-1+\tfrac12=0$;
$\Lambda(P_{k+1})(t_0,t_1)=-1$.

\emph{(d) $(\mathrm{down})$ at a Max vertex.} At $y=v_1$ the three values
$w^\ast(v_1),w^\ast(A^{1}_1),w^\ast(B^{1}_1)$ are $\tfrac12$, so it suffices
that $h_{k+1}(\theta_x-e_1)\le\min(h_k(\theta_x-\lambda e_2),
h_k(\theta_x-\beta e_1))$, and pointwise on $\Xi_{k+1}\subseteq\Xi_k$,
$\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle-\xi_1\le\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle-\lambda\xi_2$
and $\le\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle-\beta\xi_1$; $y=v_2$ is symmetric. At
$y=v_0$, $\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle-\xi_0=\gamma+\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle
-\beta_0\xi_1$, so $\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,v_0)\le\Lambda(P_k)(x,d_1)$, and it is
$\le\Lambda(P_k)(x,H)$ because $\gamma-\beta_0\xi_1\le0$ for $\xi_1\ge\ell$.

\emph{(e) $(\mathrm{up})$ at $v_0$.} $\Lambda(P_{k+1})(v_0,y)
=\tfrac12-w^\ast(y)-\gamma+h_{k+1}(\beta_0e_1-\theta_y)\le\Lambda(P_k)(d_1,y)$,
one of the two terms of the clause.

\emph{(f) $(\mathrm{up})$ at $v_1$ and $v_2$}: the only non-automatic
condition. At $v_1$ it reduces to
$h_{k+1}(e_1-\theta_y)\le\max\bigl(h_k(\lambda e_2-\theta_y),
h_k(\beta e_1-\theta_y)\bigr)$, and by \Cref{lem:cv-lift} there are four
cases. $\theta_y=0$: left $\lambda M_k$, right $\beta M_k$. $\theta_y=c\,e_1$: left
$(1-c)\lambda M_k$, and $(\xi_1,\xi_2)=(\lambda M_k,M_k)$ gives
$h_k(\lambda e_2-c\,e_1)\ge(1-c)\lambda M_k$. $\theta_y=c\,e_2$: since
$\xi_2\ge\lambda\xi_1$, the left side is at most $(1-c\lambda)\lambda M_k$,
while $(\xi_1,\xi_2)=(M_k,\lambda M_k)$ gives
$h_k(\beta e_1-c\,e_2)\ge(\beta-c\lambda)M_k$, and
$(\beta-c\lambda)-(1-c\lambda)\lambda=u\lambda(1-c)\ge0$. $\theta_y=e_0$:
$h_{k+1}(e_1-e_0)=\gamma+(1-\beta_0)M_{k+1}=\gamma+2\gamma\lambda M_k$ and
$(\xi_1,\xi_2)=(\lambda M_k,M_k)$ gives $h_k(\lambda e_2-e_0)\ge
\gamma+\lambda M_k(1-\beta_0)$. At $v_2$ three cases are verbatim with
$e_1,e_2$ exchanged, and $\theta_y=e_0$ reads
$h_{k+1}(e_2-e_0)=\gamma+\lambda M_k(1-\beta_0\lambda)$ against
$h_k(\beta e_2-e_0)\ge\gamma+M_k(\beta-\beta_0\lambda)$, with
$(\beta-\beta_0\lambda)-\lambda(1-\beta_0\lambda)=u\lambda(1-\beta_0)\ge0$.

\emph{(g) Max--Max composites.} $\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,y)\le
\max_i\Lambda(P_k)(x^{(i)},y)$ by (e)/(f), and
$\Lambda(P_k)(x^{(i)},y)\le\min_j\Lambda(P_k)(x^{(i)},y^{(j)})$ by the
pointwise argument of (d) read at the single level $k$ --- $\xi_1\ge\lambda\xi_2$,
$\xi_1\ge\beta\xi_1$ and $\xi_0=\beta_0\xi_1-\gamma\ge0$ hold on $\Xi_k$ itself,
so $\Lambda(P_m)(x,y)\le\min_j\Lambda(P_m)(x,y^{(j)})$ for every $m\le K$,
every $x$ and $y\in\{v_0,v_1,v_2\}$ --- giving $\max_i\min_j$; and $\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,y)\le
\min_j\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,y^{(j)})\le\min_j\max_i\Lambda(P_k)(x^{(i)},y^{(j)})$
by (d) at level $k+1$ then (e)/(f).

So \Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier} applies. Finally
$\Lambda(P_k)(d_1,v_0)=-\gamma+h_k(\beta_0e_1-e_0)=-\gamma+\gamma=0$ since
$\beta_0\xi_1-\xi_0\equiv\gamma$ on $\Xi_k$, and
$\Lambda(P_k)(v_0,H)=h_k(e_0)=\beta_0M_k-\gamma>0$ exactly when $M_k>\ell$.
By \Cref{rem:own-successor} clause~(i) at $v_0$ needs $\Delta_k(v_0,H)\le0$ or
$\Delta_k(v_0,d_1)\le0$ and clause~(ii) needs $\Delta_k(d_1,v_0)<0$ or
$\Delta_k(H,v_0)<0$; all four are excluded. At $v_1$,
$h_k(e_1-\lambda e_2)=(1-\lambda^{2})M_k$, attained at
$(\xi_1,\xi_2)=(M_k,\lambda M_k)$, admissible for $k\le K$, and
$h_k((1-\beta)e_1)=u^{2}M_k$; so clause~(i) is excluded at $v_1$, clause~(ii)
by \Cref{thm:hybrid-sound}, and $v_2$ is symmetric. And $\lambda M_k\ge\ell$ reads
$(k+1)\ln(1/\lambda)\le\ln(\beta_0/2\gamma)$, with
$\ln(1/\lambda)=-\ln(1-2^{-e})\le 1/(2^{e}-1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The class contains a hybrid stall]\label{cor:cv-class}
For every fixed $s\ge3$ the family $\{\mathrm{CV}(e,s)\}_{e\ge1}$ consists of
stopping SSGs with $D(G)=2^{s}=O(1)$ --- so \Cref{thm:few-denominator} solves
every member in $O(N^{2})$ integer operations --- carrying a
value-distinguishing Max vertex of gap $1/D(G)$ at which --- indeed at
every controlled vertex --- the Z-seeded own-successor hybrid of
\Cref{def:hybrid} is silent for $2^{\Omega(N)}$ rounds.
This answers the question left open after \Cref{prop:fv-stall} in the
affirmative: for the hybrid, hence (by domination, \Cref{thm:hybrid-sound})
for the slack calculus, its transitive closure and the transport test
simultaneously; it strictly strengthens
\Cref{prop:fv-stall}, whose stalls are vacuous. The multiplicative calculus
of \Cref{def:ratio}, which the additive certificate does not cover, is
\emph{not} stalled: it fires at $v_0$ within a few dozen rounds
(\Cref{prop:cv-measured}).
\end{corollary}

\begin{lemma}[The hybrid fires as soon as the polytope is narrow]\label{lem:hybrid-narrow}
Let $G$ be a stopping SSG with $\Vmin=\emptyset$, $C:=\Vmax$, and put
$M_k:=\max\{z(x)-w^\ast(x):z\in K(\Delta_k),\ x\in V\}$. Then $M_k\ge0$, the
maximum is attained on $C$, and for every $v\in\Vmax$ with non-optimal
successor $b$ and gap $\gamma_v>0$: if $M_k<\gamma_v$ then
$\Delta_{k+1}(b,v)<0$, so clause~(ii) of \Cref{rem:own-successor} fires at $v$
at round $k+1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Every $z\in Q(G)$ satisfies $z\ge Tz$, so $z\ge w^\ast$ by the minimality
clause of \Cref{thm:lfp-general}(b); with $\xi:=z-w^\ast\ge0$, a non-controlled
$u$ has $\xi_u=\sum_{w\in C}P^{u}_w\xi_w\le\max_C\xi$
(\Cref{lem:transport-dim}). Since $K(\Delta'_k)\subseteq K(\Delta_k)$ and
$\Delta_{k+1}\le\mathcal P(\Delta'_k)=\Lambda(K(\Delta'_k))$,
$\Delta_{k+1}(b,v)\le\max_{K(\Delta_k)}(z(b)-z(v))=-\gamma_v+\max(\xi_b-\xi_v)
\le-\gamma_v+M_k<0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:hybrid-narrow}
\Cref{lem:hybrid-narrow} says what a one-player stall must do: keep $K(\Delta_k)$
wider than the gap. It also explains why the natural $\mathrm{poly}(N,D)$
upper bound fails. On families in which the vertex carrying the width of
$K(\Delta_k)$ is itself the vertex that must decide (a ``self-reading''
hypothesis of an earlier draft of this section) the recursion is
$M_{k+1}\le(\rho_bM_k-\gamma)^{+}$, an \emph{additive} decrement of at
least $\gamma\ge1/D$ per round, whence $O(D)$ rounds. It is false in
general: on $\mathrm{CV}(e,s)$ the width is $M_0=M_1=\tfrac12$ and
$M_k=\lambda^{k-1}/2$ for $k\ge1$ (measured; the certificate gives only
$M_k\ge\lambda^{k}/2$), so from the first round on the decrement is
$M_k-M_{k+1}=2^{-e}M_k$, which
drops below $\gamma$ as soon as $M_k<2^{\,e-s}$, and the last $\Theta(2^{e})$
rounds each move $M_k$ by far less than $\gamma$. The vertices that carry the
width need not be the vertices that must decide: in $\mathrm{CV}(e,s)$ the
width lives on the vacuous pair $(v_1,v_2)$, where there is no gap to subtract.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[What the family separates]\label{rem:cv}
The pair $(v_1,v_2)$ is a \emph{flat} core --- both successors of each have
value $\tfrac12$, so both vertices are vacuous in the sense of
\Cref{prop:fv-stall} and the core alone has $D=2$ --- and $v_0$ is a
\emph{reader} whose non-optimal successor reads $v_1$ with weight
$\beta_0=1-2\gamma$. The core is exactly $CC(L,m)$ of \Cref{thm:hybrid-lower}
with its leak deleted: there $B^{i}$ ends at $d_1$ of value
$\tfrac12-2^{-2L-m}$, which makes $v_1,v_2$ value-distinguishing with an
exponentially small gap, puts $CC$ outside the class, and makes the
own-successor entry $\Delta_1(B_1,v_1)=-\gamma$ negative at once
(\Cref{rem:hybrid-lower-not-a-refutation}). Ending $B^{i}$ at $H$ instead makes
that gap $0$, so the negative entry disappears and the core becomes
uninformative rather than decided; the decision is then moved to a vertex whose
gap is coarse. Separating \emph{who is slow} from \emph{who must decide} is
what turns \Cref{thm:hybrid-lower} into a genuine stall. Two readings should be
resisted. First, this says nothing about \Cref{prob:main}:
$\mathrm{CV}(e,s)$ is solved by \Cref{cor:grid-iteration} on the grid
$2^{-s}\mathbb Z$ in $O(N^{2}2^{s})$ operations, $O(N^{2})$ at fixed $s$, and
its all-switches runs have length $\le1$. What
it shows is that the hybrid is blind to precision structure the rounded
iteration sees. Second, $\mathrm{CV}$ does not defeat $M1$ or $M3$:
$v_0\preceq H$ holds in \Cref{def:simorder}, and all-switches halts within one
productive round, so $M(1,0)$ of \Cref{def:seeded} decides at once --- and by
\Cref{thm:seed-dichotomy} the latter is unavoidable short of a superpolynomial
all-switches family.
 Against the wedge of \Cref{sec:wedge} the comparison is this: $\mathrm{WD}(2j,j,j+4)$
already keeps the same Z-seeded own-successor hybrid silent for
$2^{\Omega(N)}$ rounds at a value-distinguishing Max vertex
(\Cref{thm:wedge-proved,cor:wedge-count}), defeating the same additive
mechanisms and failing against the same two --- the simulation preorder at
round zero, and the one-round variant of the seeded calculus --- and the
wedge stalls the multiplicative calculus of \Cref{def:ratio} as well, where
$\mathrm{CV}$ does not (\Cref{prop:cv-measured}); what
$\mathrm{CV}$ adds is the \emph{coarse gap}: the wedge's deciding gap is
$2^{-\Theta(N)}$, here it is $1/D$ with $D$ constant, and the family lies
inside the class of \Cref{thm:few-denominator}. The mechanism is the
separation of the vertex that is slow (the vacuous pair, where the width
of $K(\Delta_k)$ lives and there is no gap to subtract) from the vertex
that must decide (the reader $v_0$).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Measured]\label{prop:cv-measured}
In exact rational arithmetic, with the free $Z$-seed and both directions of
\Cref{rem:own-successor} at $v_0$ --- the only value-distinguishing vertex ---
the first firing round on $\mathrm{CV}(e,s)$ is
\[
 \begin{array}{l|ccccc}
   e\qquad (s=4,\ D=16,\ N=6e+9) & 1&2&3&4&5\\\hline
   N                             & 15&21&27&33&39\\
   \text{hybrid }M5              & 4&8&16&32&63\\
   \Cref{thm:cv-lower}\ \text{(proved)} & 2&6&14&30&61\\
   \Cref{def:slack}\ M2          & 12&24&47&185&744\\
   \Cref{def:trans-slack}\ M2T   & 11&23&47&95&189\\
   \text{first }k\text{ with }u_k(v_0)-l_k(v_0)<\gamma & 16&60&240&961&3847
 \end{array}
\]
and at $s=3$ ($D=8$, $N=6e+8$) the hybrid takes $3,5,10,19,36$ at
$e=1,\dots,5$ against the proved $1,3,8,17,34$, while $M2$ takes
$10,16,56,214,846$ and $M2T$ takes $8,16,31,60,114$. So at a \emph{fixed}
denominator the hybrid and $M2T$ double with every six extra vertices, the
value-iteration bracket quadruples, and $M2$ doubles up to $e=3$ and
quadruples from there. The one-shot transport test of
\Cref{def:transport} is \emph{permanently} silent:
$\operatorname{Sep}(v_0,H)=\operatorname{Sep}(v_0,d_1)=0$ at every $e$ and $s$
tested. The multiplicative calculus of \Cref{def:ratio} is the one
propagating mechanism the family does not stall: with both clauses of
\Cref{rem:own-successor} it fires at $v_0$ through clause~(ii),
$R_k(d_1,v_0)<1$, at rounds $12,22,39,74,141$ at $s=4$ ($e=1,\dots,5$)
and $10,16,26,44$ at $s=3$ ($e=1,\dots,4$) --- between two and three times
the hybrid's count, doubling with it, and far below $M2$'s
(computed twice, from two independent implementations of \Cref{def:ratio}).
At $v_1$ and $v_2$ the hybrid never fires, by \Cref{thm:cv-lower} itself.
Against this, the
grid-rounded calculus $M7$ fires at $v_0$ at round $9$ at $s=4$; the simulation
preorder of \Cref{def:simorder} fires at round zero; and all-switches takes $0$
or $1$ productive rounds and \Cref{def:bsi} likewise, from each of the eight
starts. The certificate of \Cref{thm:cv-lower} was verified entrywise ---
$P_k\subseteq Q(G)$, nesting, and all $N^{2}$ clause inequalities at every
level --- at the $24$ pairs $(e,s)\in\{1,\dots,6\}\times\{3,4,5,6\}$ and, on
freshly generated larger instances, at $(7,4)$, $(8,3)$ and $(7,6)$, where it
certifies $248$, $280$ and $437$ silent rounds at $N=51,56,53$ and
$D=16,8,64$. The clause checker was validated against the harness's independent
implementation of \Cref{def:slack} on $200$ random games in both directions and
reproduces the published certificate of \Cref{thm:hybrid-lower} on $CC(1,2)$,
$CC(2,2)$, $CC(1,4)$ and $CC(2,3)$. The hybrid counts come from a
vertex-enumeration engine in the three controlled coordinates which reproduces
$\mathrm{fasthyb}$ on five $CW(e,s)$, and were reproduced by the harness's
linear-programming hybrid on $\mathrm{CV}(1,3)$ and $\mathrm{CV}(1,4)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Stopping and one-player are immediate: every out-edge leaves a chain towards a
sink or towards $v_1,v_2$, and each chain ends in a sink or in $d_1$, whose own
chain ends in the sinks; so no set closed as in \Cref{lem:trapchar} avoids the
sinks. For the values, put $X:=\val(v_1)$, $Y:=\val(v_2)$. Along
$\mathrm{ch}(l,b,z)$ the token leaves to $b$ at each of the $l$ steps with
probability $\tfrac12$, so the first vertex has value
$(1-2^{-l})\val(b)+2^{-l}\val(z)$. With $\val(e)=\tfrac12$ and
$\val(d_1)=\tfrac12-2^{-m}$ --- the latter because $\val(d_j)=1-2^{-(m-j+1)}$
for $j\ge2$ by backward induction and $\val(d_1)=\tfrac12\val(d_2)$ --- this
gives $\val(A_1)=(1-2^{-L})Y+2^{-L}\tfrac12$ and
$\val(B_1)=(1-2^{-2L})X+2^{-2L}(\tfrac12-2^{-m})$, and symmetrically. Setting
$X=Y=\tfrac12$ satisfies $X=\max(\val(A_1),\val(B_1))$ since then
$\val(A_1)=\tfrac12$ and $\val(B_1)=\tfrac12-\gamma$; uniqueness is
\Cref{thm:contraction}.

The affine form is the one used in \Cref{prop:hybrid-onectrl}, now with two
parameters: making $v_1,v_2$ and the sinks absorbing leaves an absorbing average
system, so $z(x)=\langle\theta_x,s\rangle+\rho_x$ with $s=(z(v_1),z(v_2))$,
$\theta_{A_1}=(0,\lambda)$, $\theta_{B_1}=(\beta,0)$, $\theta_{A_2}=(\lambda,0)$,
$\theta_{B_2}=(0,\beta)$ for $\beta:=1-2^{-2L}$, and every $\theta_x$ of the form
$(0,0)$, $(a,0)$ or $(0,a)$ with $a\in\{1-2^{-i}:1\le i\le2L\}\cup\{1\}$. The
support function of $\Xi_k$ is
$h_k(c)=M_k\max\{0,c_1+\lambda c_2,c_1+c_2,\lambda c_1+c_2\}$, so
$\Lambda(P_k)(x,y)=\val(x)-\val(y)+h_k(\theta_x-\theta_y)$.

That $P_k\subseteq Q(CC(L,m))$ is a finite check on the four vertices of $\Xi_k$:
the Max rows read $\xi_1\ge\lambda\xi_2$, which defines the cone, and
$(1-\beta)\xi_1\ge-\gamma$, which is vacuous; the box rows hold because a vertex
with $\theta_x=0$ has $z(x)=\val(x)$, and one with $\theta_x\ne0$ has
$\val(x)\le\tfrac12$ and $0\le\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle\le M_k\le\tfrac12$. For
the clause condition, the base clauses hold because $P_k\subseteq\mathcal X$;
at an average vertex $\theta_x$ and $\val(x)$ are both the mean over the two
successors, so sublinearity of $h_k$ makes the $(\mathrm{up})$, $(\mathrm{down})$
and average composites automatic; and at $v_1$ the $(\mathrm{up})$ clause
requires $\max\bigl(h_k(\theta_{A_1}-\theta_y),\,h_k(\theta_{B_1}-\theta_y)-\gamma\bigr)
\ge h_{k+1}(e_1-\theta_y)$ for every $y$, whose binding case is $y=v_2$ and reads
exactly $M_{k+1}\le\lambda M_k-\gamma/u$. The $(\mathrm{down})$ clause and the
four same-kind pairs among $v_1,v_2$ are checked the same way; at $(v_1,v_2)$
both composites are at least $u(1-u)M_k-\gamma$, which is $uM_{k+1}$, and
$\Lambda(P_{k+1})(v_1,v_2)=uM_{k+1}$ exactly.

Finally $\theta_{B_1}-\theta_{A_1}=(\beta,-\lambda)$ gives
$h_k(\beta,-\lambda)=(\beta-\lambda^2)M_k=2u(1-u)M_k$, so
$\Lambda(P_k)(B_1,A_1)=-\gamma+2u(1-u)M_k$, which is nonnegative exactly while
$M_k\ge\gamma/(2u(1-u))=2^{-L-m-1}/\lambda$. Solving
$\lambda^{k}(\tfrac12+2^{-m})-2^{-m}>2^{-L-m-1}/\lambda$ and using
$\lambda\ge\tfrac12$ gives the displayed range, and
$\ln(1/\lambda)=-\ln(1-2^{-L})\le1/(2^{L}-1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:hybrid-lower-verified}
We verified the certificate itself, clause by clause and in exact rational
arithmetic: for each $k$, that every vertex of $P_k$ lies in $Q(CC(L,m))$, that
$P_{k+1}\subseteq P_k$, and that the minimum over all clauses of
\Cref{def:slack} evaluated at $\Lambda(P_k)$ dominates $\Lambda(P_{k+1})$ at all
$N^{2}$ pairs. There was no failure, and the guaranteed-undecided round counts --- the
largest $k$ with $\Lambda(P_k)(B_i,A_i)\ge0$, that is with
$M_k\ge2^{-L-m-1}/\lambda$; an earlier version of this table printed the
last $k$ with $M_k>0$, one larger in six of the ten cells --- are
\[
  \begin{array}{l|ccccc}
    L & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4 & 5\\\hline
    m=2\ (N=13,19,25,31,37) & 1 & 3 & 7 & 16 & 34\\
    m=4\ (N=15,21,27,33,39) & 2 & 7 & 15 & 33 & 68
  \end{array}
\]
--- doubling with every unit increase of $L$, that is with every six extra
vertices. Running the hybrid itself on $CC(1,2)$ decides at round $3$, against
the certificate's guarantee of $1$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:hybrid-rate}
Where this leaves the hybrid. \Cref{prop:hybrid-rate} reduces its rate to the
decay of the box width $\omega_k$; \Cref{thm:hybrid-lower} shows that the
natural certificate can be exponentially slow on the entries
$\Delta_k(v^{(0)},v^{(1)})$; and \Cref{rem:hybrid-lower-not-a-refutation} shows
that those are not the entries a decision rule uses. The mechanism is therefore
\emph{not} refuted, and the rate question is open.

Two things are worth separating out from the attempt. First, why our
measurements were uninformative. Every instance on which this document could
measure the hybrid before $CC$ --- $G_8$, $H_m$, $S$ and the whole family $S_r$
--- has exactly one controlled vertex, and \Cref{prop:hybrid-onectrl} shows that
class is decided at round two unconditionally. So
\Cref{prop:hybrid-decides} measures that proposition and nothing else. The
lesson generalises beyond this section: before believing a measurement, identify
the parameter the mechanism turns on --- here $|\Vmax|+|\Vmin|$ --- and check
that the instance set varies it. Ours did not vary it at all.

Second, what $CC$ needs that $W$ and $S_r$ do not have. On $S_r$ the single Max
vertex has a successor of \emph{pinned} value, $\val(v)\ge\val(a)=\tfrac12$,
which fixes the lower end of the box at round one; that is the whole reason the
family collapses in two rounds. $CC$ removes every such pin by making each Max
vertex compare two chains that both return to the controlled pair, and it uses
\emph{two} Max vertices wired crosswise, so that the uncertainty at $v_1$ is
resupplied by $v_2$ and conversely. The cone $\xi_1\ge\lambda\xi_2$,
$\xi_2\ge\lambda\xi_1$ of \Cref{thm:hybrid-lower} is exactly that mutual
resupply, and $\lambda=1-2^{-L}$ is the probability of returning to the other
Max vertex rather than escaping down a chain. One Max vertex cannot do this: by
\Cref{prop:hybrid-onectrl} there is nothing to resupply it. Two constructions
that lack the crosswise wiring fail accordingly: lengthening a single cycle
joining a Max vertex to a Min vertex, with each chain leaking back into the
cycle and one sink escape, so that the return probability is
$1-2^{-\Theta(k)}$ --- the shape of \Cref{thm:impedance} --- gives a family whose
values are $\tfrac13$ and $\tfrac23$ for every $k$ and whose box collapses at
round $2$ for every $k$, because the programme solves the whole cycle at a
stroke once the two choices are read as inequalities; and having one Max vertex
compare two such cycles slows the collapse to $k+1$ rounds but leaves the first
decision at round $2$.

For reference, the slowest instance a hill climb over small games produced is
the nine-vertex stopping game $W$ on $\{0,\dots,6\}$ with $t_0=7$, $t_1=8$,
$1\in\Vmin$, $5\in\Vmax$, the rest average and
\[
  0\to(t_0,2),\quad 1\to(3,2),\quad 2\to(5,3),\quad 3\to(4,6),\quad
  4\to(5,1),\quad 5\to(4,0),\quad 6\to(t_1,t_0);
\]
it has $\val=(\tfrac14,\tfrac12,\tfrac12,\tfrac12,\tfrac12,\tfrac12,\tfrac12)$
and its box collapses at round $3$, with widths $\tfrac{10}{11},\tfrac15,0$.
Random and hill-climbed search over games of this size never exceeded three
rounds (a report: neither the two prose constructions above nor the search
are printed). As everywhere else in this document, whatever is hard has to
be engineered.
\end{remark}


\subsection{A one-player family the whole propagation side loses on}\label{sec:bc}

Every stall recorded so far --- $H_m$, $\mathrm{CC}$, $\mathrm{WD}$,
$\mathrm{CV}$ --- is decided at round zero by the simulation preorder M1,
and $\mathrm{CV}$ is decided by the ratio calculus M6 within a few dozen
rounds (\Cref{prop:cv-measured}). The round-$17$ oracle-barrier route
built a one-player family on which nothing on the propagation side fires:
at its unique value-distinguishing vertex the pair carries the fooling
frame of $G_8$, so no sound local matching calculus (M1 included) derives
it, and three Max vertices carry a wedge shrinking by $1-\tfrac32\cdot2^{-e}$
per round, so a convex-certificate chain keeps the slack calculus, its
closure, the transport step, the hybrid and the ratio calculus silent for
$2^{\Omega(N)}$ rounds, in both registers of
\Cref{thm:convex-barrier-both}. One exact policy evaluation decides it,
as \Cref{thm:seed-dichotomy} predicts: all-switches converges on it in one
round. It is, like every family here, easy --- one player,
\Cref{thm:one-player} --- and its denominator is $2^{\Theta(e)}$, so unlike
$\mathrm{CV}$ it lies outside \Cref{thm:few-denominator}'s class.

\begin{definition}[$\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$]\label{def:bc}
For $e\ge2$, $s\ge5$ put $u:=2^{-e}$, $\gamma:=2^{-s}$. Let
$\mathrm{CQ},\mathrm{CE},\mathrm{CH},\mathrm{Q}_0$ be the dyadic sink
chains of \Cref{lem:dyadic-row} realising the constants
$\tfrac14,\tfrac38,\tfrac34,\tfrac12+2\gamma$, of lengths $2,3,2,s-1$;
every vertex of them is average and reads one sink and the next vertex
of its chain (\Cref{lem:dyadic-row}).
$\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$ has $\Vmax=\{v_0,v_1,v_2\}$, $\Vmin=\emptyset$, all
other non-sinks average, and, with $o:=3-i$,
$\mathrm{HI}_1:=t_1$, $\mathrm{HI}_2:=\mathrm{CH}$,
$\mathrm{LO}_1:=\mathrm{CQ}$, $\mathrm{LO}_2:=\mathrm{CE}$:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\alpha^{i}_q\to(v_{o},\alpha^{i}_{q+1})\ (q<e), &&\alpha^{i}_{e}\to(v_{o},\mathrm{HI}_i),\\
&A^{i}_1\to(\alpha^{i}_1,A^{i}_2),&&
A^{i}_q\to(v_{o},A^{i}_{q+1})\ (2\le q<e),\qquad A^{i}_{e}\to(v_{o},\mathrm{LO}_i),\\
&\beta^{i}_q\to(v_{i},\beta^{i}_{q+1})\ (q<2e),&&\beta^{i}_{2e}\to(v_{i},\mathrm{HI}_i),\\
&B^{i}_1\to(\beta^{i}_1,B^{i}_2),&&
B^{i}_q\to(v_{i},B^{i}_{q+1})\ (2\le q<2e),\qquad B^{i}_{2e}\to(v_{i},\mathrm{LO}_i),\\
&v_i\to(A^{i}_1,B^{i}_1)\ (i=1,2),&&\\
&g\to(g,t_1),\qquad P\to(g,t_0),&&\\
&D_q\to(v_1,D_{q+1})\ (q<s-3),&&D_{s-3}\to(v_1,t_0),\\
&Q\to(\mathrm{Q}_0,D_1),\qquad v_0\to(Q,P),&&
\end{aligned}
\]
with start vertex $v_0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Basic data]\label{prop:bc-values}
$\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$ is a stopping one-player SSG with $N=12e+2s+11$.
Put $\eta_1:=\tfrac12$, $\eta_2:=\tfrac14$ (so
$\eta_i=w^\ast(\mathrm{HI}_i)-\tfrac12$) and $\zeta_i:=\tfrac12\eta_i$
(so $\zeta_i=\tfrac12-w^\ast(\mathrm{LO}_i)$). Then
\[
\begin{aligned}
&w^\ast(v_0)=w^\ast(v_1)=w^\ast(v_2)=w^\ast(P)=w^\ast(A^{i}_1)=w^\ast(B^{i}_1)=\tfrac12,
\qquad w^\ast(g)=1,\qquad w^\ast(Q)=\tfrac12-\gamma,\\
&w^\ast(\alpha^{i}_q)=\tfrac12+2^{-(e-q+1)}\eta_i,\qquad
 w^\ast(A^{i}_q)=\tfrac12-2^{-(e-q+1)}\zeta_i\quad(q\ge2),\\
&w^\ast(\beta^{i}_q)=\tfrac12+2^{-(2e-q+1)}\eta_i,\qquad
 w^\ast(B^{i}_q)=\tfrac12-2^{-(2e-q+1)}\zeta_i\quad(q\ge2),\qquad
 w^\ast(D_q)=\tfrac12-2^{-(s-1-q)} .
\end{aligned}
\]
Hence $Z_0=\{t_0\}$, $Z_1=\{g,t_1\}$, and $v_0$ is the \emph{unique}
value-distinguishing controlled vertex, with $w^\ast(P)-w^\ast(Q)=\gamma$
and $P$ optimal. The lift for $C=\{v_0,v_1,v_2\}$, in coordinates
$(e_0,e_1,e_2)$, is $\theta_{v_j}=e_j$, $\theta=0$ at $g$, $P$, the two
sinks and every dyadic chain, and
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\theta_{\alpha^{i}_q}=\bigl(1-2^{-(e-q+1)}\bigr)e_{o}\ (q\ge1),\quad
 \theta_{A^{i}_q}=\bigl(1-2^{-(e-q+1)}\bigr)e_{o}\ (q\ge2),
&&\theta_{A^{i}_1}=a\,e_{o},\\
&\theta_{\beta^{i}_q}=\bigl(1-2^{-(2e-q+1)}\bigr)e_{i}\ (q\ge1),\quad
 \theta_{B^{i}_q}=\bigl(1-2^{-(2e-q+1)}\bigr)e_{i}\ (q\ge2),
&&\theta_{B^{i}_1}=b\,e_{i},\\
&\theta_{D_q}=\bigl(1-2^{-(s-2-q)}\bigr)e_1,&&\theta_Q=c\,e_1,
\end{aligned}
\qquad
a:=1-\tfrac32u,\quad b:=1-\tfrac32u^{2},\quad c:=\tfrac12-4\gamma .
\]
Every $\theta_x$ is $0$, $e_0$, or $c'e_1$ or $c'e_2$ with
$c'\in(0,1]$; every $y$ with $\theta_y=c'e_2$ has
$w^\ast(y)\ge\tfrac14+\tfrac{c'}4$, and every $y$ with $\theta_y=c'e_1$
has $w^\ast(y)\ge\tfrac{c'}2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Counting: $3+3+\bigl(2+3+2+(s-1)\bigr)+2\cdot6e+(s-3)=12e+2s+9$
non-sinks. Stopping (\Cref{lem:trapchar}): every dyadic chain vertex has
a sink successor, so lies outside any trap $U$; then
$\alpha^{i}_e,\beta^{i}_{2e}\notin U$, inductively $\alpha^{i}_1,
\beta^{i}_1\notin U$, hence the average vertices $A^{i}_1,B^{i}_1\notin U$,
hence $v_1,v_2\notin U$ (no successor inside); then $D_{s-3}$, every
$D_q$, $Q$, $g$, $P$ and $v_0$. So $U=\emptyset$.

The displayed vector is a fixed point of $T$. On the dyadic chains this is
\Cref{lem:dyadic-row}; $w^\ast(g)=\tfrac12(1+1)$ and $w^\ast(P)=\tfrac12(1+0)$.
Along $\alpha^{i}$, writing $y_q:=w^\ast(\alpha^{i}_q)-\tfrac12$, the last
vertex gives $y_e=\tfrac12\bigl(w^\ast(\mathrm{HI}_i)-\tfrac12\bigr)=\tfrac12\eta_i$
and $y_q=\tfrac12y_{q+1}$, which is the display; the same computation on
$\beta^{i}$, and on $A^{i}$, $B^{i}$ for $q\ge2$ with
$-\tfrac12\zeta_i$ in place of $\tfrac12\eta_i$. Then
\[
  w^\ast(A^{i}_1)=\tfrac12\bigl(w^\ast(\alpha^{i}_1)+w^\ast(A^{i}_2)\bigr)
  =\tfrac12+\tfrac12\bigl(2^{-e}\eta_i-2^{-(e-1)}\zeta_i\bigr)=\tfrac12
\]
because $\eta_i=2\zeta_i$, and identically $w^\ast(B^{i}_1)=\tfrac12$;
so $w^\ast(v_i)=\max(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)=\tfrac12$. Backwards from
$w^\ast(D_{s-3})=\tfrac12(\tfrac12+0)=\tfrac14$ gives the $D$-chain,
whence $w^\ast(D_1)=\tfrac12-2^{-(s-2)}=\tfrac12-4\gamma$ and
$w^\ast(Q)=\tfrac12\bigl((\tfrac12+2\gamma)+(\tfrac12-4\gamma)\bigr)
=\tfrac12-\gamma$, and $w^\ast(v_0)=\max(\tfrac12-\gamma,\tfrac12)=\tfrac12$.
Since $G$ is stopping, \Cref{cor:comparison} identifies this fixed point
with $w^\ast$. The qualitative sets are read off: only $t_0$ has value
$0$, only $g$ and $t_1$ have value $1$. $v_1$ and $v_2$ are not
value-distinguishing; $v_0$ is, with gap $\gamma$.

The lift is the same backward substitution with $\theta_{v_j}=e_j$; a
chain of $r$ average vertices reading $v_j$ and ending at a
$\theta$-free vertex carries $\theta=(1-2^{-r})e_j$ at its head, and
$\theta_{A^{i}_1}=\tfrac12\bigl((1-2^{-e})+(1-2^{-(e-1)})\bigr)e_o
=(1-\tfrac32u)e_o$, $\theta_{B^{i}_1}=(1-\tfrac32u^{2})e_i$,
$\theta_Q=\tfrac12\bigl(0+(1-2^{-(s-3)})\bigr)e_1=(\tfrac12-4\gamma)e_1$.

Last sentence. A vertex $y$ with $\theta_y=c'e_2$, $c'>0$, is one of
$v_2$, $\alpha^{1}_\bullet$, $A^{1}_\bullet$, $\beta^{2}_\bullet$,
$B^{2}_\bullet$; its value is $\tfrac{c'}2+(1-c')\tau_y$ where $\tau_y$
is a convex combination of the terminal values it reaches, namely
$1,\tfrac34,\tfrac14,\tfrac38$, all $\ge\tfrac14$, so
$w^\ast(y)\ge\tfrac{c'}2+\tfrac{1-c'}4=\tfrac14+\tfrac{c'}4$. A vertex
with $\theta_y=c'e_1$ may in addition reach the terminal $t_0$ (along
$D$), so only $w^\ast(y)\ge\tfrac{c'}2$ is available --- and it is
attained, $w^\ast(D_q)=\tfrac12\theta_{D_q}$ exactly.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[No sound local matching calculus decides
$v_0$]\label{prop:bc-lmc}
In $\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$ the pair $(Q,P)$ carries the frame of the pair
$(4,1)$ of $G_8$ (\Cref{prop:simorder-stalls,lem:fooling-partner}) and the
pair $(v_0,P)$ carries the frame of $(5,1)$, in each case with the same
set of value-true atoms. Hence no sound local matching calculus
(\Cref{def:lmc}) derives $(Q,P)$ or $(v_0,P)$, in either reading; and
$(v_0,Q)$, $(P,Q)$ are value-false. So no sound local matching calculus
decides $v_0$ by any of the four tests of \Cref{rem:own-successor}
available to an order-only mechanism. Moreover the preorder $\preceq$ of
\Cref{def:simorder} decides neither $v_1$ nor $v_2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The frames. Writing $x,x^{(0)},x^{(1)},y,y^{(0)},y^{(1)}$ for the six
symbols, $(Q,P)$ interprets them at $Q,\mathrm{Q}_0,D_1,P,g,t_0$, of types
$(\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},t_0)$,
all six distinct, with value tuple
$(\tfrac12-\gamma,\tfrac12+2\gamma,\tfrac12-4\gamma;\tfrac12,1,0)$; the
value-true atoms are exactly
$\Pi=\{(x,y^{(0)}),(x^{(0)},y^{(0)}),(x^{(1)},y),(x^{(1)},y^{(0)})\}$,
which is what \Cref{lem:fooling-partner} computes at $(4,1)$ of $G_8$
(value tuple $(\tfrac25,\tfrac35,\tfrac15;\tfrac12,1,0)$) and at
$(a_0,d)$ of $G_{\mathrm f}$. The proof of \Cref{thm:matching-barrier}
applies verbatim: a sound clause deriving $(Q,P)$ is contained in $\Pi$,
and the same clause derives $(a_0,d)$ in $G_{\mathrm f}$, where
$w^\ast(a_0)>w^\ast(d)$.

$(v_0,P)$ interprets the symbols at $v_0,Q,P,P,g,t_0$, of types
$(\max,\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},t_0)$, with
equality pattern $\{x^{(1)}=y\}$ and value-true atoms
$\Pi\cup\{(x^{(0)},y)\}$, the extra atom interpreting as $(Q,P)$. That is
precisely the frame, equality pattern and true-atom set of $(5,1)$ in
$G_8$. Since $(Q,P)$ is underivable, a clause deriving $(v_0,P)$ is
contained in $\Pi$, and the game $K_\sharp$ of \Cref{thm:matching-barrier}
fools every such clause. (The atom $(x^{(1)},y)$ interprets there as the
diagonal pair $(P,P)$, whose frame --- types
$(\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},t_0\,|\,\mathrm{avg},\mathrm{avg},t_0)$,
equality pattern $\{x=y,\,x^{(0)}=y^{(0)},\,x^{(1)}=y^{(1)}\}$, the same
true atoms --- is that of $(1,1)$ in $G_8$ and of $(k,k)$ in $K_\sharp$,
so the transfer is complete; the route's auditor.)

For $\preceq$ at $v_1$ (and symmetrically $v_2$) put
$\mathcal S_0:=\mathcal V:=\{(x,y):w^\ast(x)\le w^\ast(y)\}$ and
$\mathcal S_{j+1}:=\mathcal V\cap\mathcal F(\mathcal S_j)$; since
$\preceq\subseteq\mathcal V$ (\Cref{thm:simorder-sound}) and
$\preceq=\mathcal F(\preceq)$, induction gives
$\preceq\subseteq\mathcal S_j$ for every $j$. Write
$\rho:=2^{-e}\eta_1$ and $\rho':=2^{-2e}\eta_1$, so that the successors of
$A^{1}_1$ have values $\tfrac12\pm\rho$ and those of $B^{1}_1$ have values
$\tfrac12\pm\rho'$, with $0<\rho'<\rho$. Then
$(B^{1}_1,A^{1}_1)\notin\mathcal S_1$: the (down) clause at $A^{1}_1$
needs $(B^{1}_1,A^{1}_2)$, i.e.\ $\tfrac12\le\tfrac12-\rho$; the (up)
clause at $B^{1}_1$ needs $(\beta^{1}_1,A^{1}_1)$, i.e.\
$\tfrac12+\rho'\le\tfrac12$; the (match) clause needs, for
$\pi=\mathrm{id}$, $(B^{1}_2,A^{1}_2)$, i.e.\
$\tfrac12-\rho'\le\tfrac12-\rho$, false since $\rho'<\rho$, and for the
transposition $(\beta^{1}_1,A^{1}_2)$, comparing a value above $\tfrac12$
with one below. Symmetrically $(A^{1}_1,B^{1}_1)\notin\mathcal S_1$, the
$\pi=\mathrm{id}$ branch failing at $(\alpha^{1}_1,\beta^{1}_1)$, i.e.\ at
$\tfrac12+\rho\le\tfrac12+\rho'$. Hence
$(v_1,A^{1}_1)\notin\mathcal S_2$: its (up) clause needs
$(B^{1}_1,A^{1}_1)\in\mathcal S_1$, its (down) clause needs
$(v_1,A^{1}_2)\in\mathcal V$, i.e.\ $\tfrac12\le\tfrac12-\rho$, and no
match clause applies, the types differing; likewise
$(v_1,B^{1}_1)\notin\mathcal S_2$. The remaining two tests at $v_1$ are
the pair tests, which are the two pairs just excluded.

Two remarks on the shape of the construction. First, the two halves of the
engine must carry \emph{different} straddle amplitudes: if
$\eta_1=\eta_2$ the match clause relates $A^{1}_1$ with $A^{2}_1$ and
$B^{1}_1$ with $B^{2}_1$ in both directions, and then
$v_1\preceq v_2\preceq v_1$, so $\preceq$ contributes the cuts
$z(v_1)=z(v_2)$; those cuts collapse $\Xi_k$ of \Cref{thm:bc-cert} to its
diagonal, and \Cref{thm:bc-lower}'s clause on seeding by $M1$ fails.
(Verified: with $\eta_1=\eta_2$ the preorder still decides no controlled
vertex, but its cuts are violated by the certificate at every round.)
Second, $\preceq$ is already transitive on $\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$ --- verified
exactly at the ten sizes of \Cref{rem:bc-measured} --- so reading
\Cref{def:simorder} with transitive closure changes nothing. For a
\emph{general} sound local matching calculus read with transitive closure
we make no claim: \Cref{thm:matching-barrier}'s closure paragraph turns on
the value multiset of $G_8$, which has only three vertices in the relevant
interval, and $\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$ has many.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[What a lifted certificate has to check]\label{lem:lift-reduction}
Let $G$ be a stopping SSG whose non-sink vertices outside a set
$C\subseteq\Vmax$ are all average, let $\theta\colon V\to\mathbb R^{C}_{\ge0}$
be the \emph{lift} --- the unique solution of
$\theta_x=\tfrac12(\theta_{x^{(0)}}+\theta_{x^{(1)}})$ at average $x$,
$\theta_v=e_v$ on $C$, $\theta_{t_0}=\theta_{t_1}=0$ --- and let
$\Xi_{K}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\Xi_0\subseteq\mathbb R^{C}_{\ge0}$ be
nonempty compact convex, with
$P_k:=\{x\mapsto w^\ast(x)+\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle:\xi\in\Xi_k\}$.
Suppose
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2.6em]
\item[$(\alpha)$] $P_k\subseteq Q(G;L_Z,U_Z)$ for every $k\le K$;
\item[$(\beta)$] for every $v\in C$, every $y\in V$ and every $k<K$,
\[
  \max_{i}\Lambda(P_k)\bigl(v^{(i)},y\bigr)\ \ge\ \Lambda(P_{k+1})(v,y)
  \qquad\text{and}\qquad
  \max_{i}\Lambda_\times(P_k)\bigl(v^{(i)},y\bigr)\ \ge\
  \Lambda_\times(P_{k+1})(v,y).
\]
\end{itemize}
Then $(P_k)_{k\le K}$ satisfies hypotheses \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)}
of \Cref{thm:convex-barrier-both}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $\Lambda_k:=\Lambda(P_k)$, $\Lambda^{\times}_k:=\Lambda_\times(P_k)$.

\emph{Average vertices.} If $x$ is average then $w^\ast(x)$ and
$\theta_x$ are the means of those of $x^{(0)},x^{(1)}$, so
$z(x)=\tfrac12(z(x^{(0)})+z(x^{(1)}))$ for every $z\in P_{k+1}$; taking
$z$ attaining $\Lambda_{k+1}(x,y)$,
$\Lambda_{k+1}(x,y)=\tfrac12\sum_i(z(x^{(i)})-z(y))
\le\tfrac12\sum_i\Lambda_{k+1}(x^{(i)},y)\le\tfrac12\sum_i\Lambda_k(x^{(i)},y)$,
the (up) clause. The (down) clause at an average $y$ and the two
average--average composites are the same computation applied to
$\theta_x-\theta_y=\tfrac12\sum_i(\theta_{x^{(i)}}-\theta_{y^{(\pi(i))}})$.
Multiplicatively, for $z$ attaining $\Lambda^\times_{k+1}(x,y)$: the (up)
clause at an average $x$ is
$\tfrac12\sum_i\Lambda^\times_k(x^{(i)},y)\ge
\tfrac12\sum_iz(x^{(i)})/z(y)=z(x)/z(y)$; the (down) clause at an average
$y$ is the harmonic mean, which is monotone, so
$\mathrm{hm}\bigl(\Lambda^\times_k(x,y^{(0)}),\Lambda^\times_k(x,y^{(1)})\bigr)
\ge\mathrm{hm}\bigl(z(x)/z(y^{(0)}),z(x)/z(y^{(1)})\bigr)
=z(x)\big/\tfrac12\textstyle\sum_jz(y^{(j)})=z(x)/z(y)$; and the
average--average composite is the mediant inequality
$z(x)/z(y)=\sum_iz(x^{(i)})\big/\sum_iz(y^{(\pi(i))})
\le\max_iz(x^{(i)})/z(y^{(\pi(i))})$.

\emph{(down) at $v\in C$.} $P_m\subseteq Q(G)$ gives $z(v)\ge z(v^{(j)})$
pointwise, hence $\Lambda_m(x,v)\le\Lambda_m(x,v^{(j)})$ and
$\Lambda^\times_m(x,v)\le\Lambda^\times_m(x,v^{(j)})$ for all $m,x,j$;
with $\Lambda_{k+1}\le\Lambda_k$,
$\min_j\Lambda_k(x,v^{(j)})\ge\Lambda_k(x,v)\ge\Lambda_{k+1}(x,v)$, which
is the clause, and likewise multiplicatively. There are no $\Vmin$
vertices, so no dual case arises.

\emph{Base clauses.} By $(\alpha)$ every $z\in P_{k+1}$ has $z(t_0)=0$,
$z(t_1)=1$, $0\le z\le1$, $z\equiv0$ on $Z_0$ and $z\equiv1$ on $Z_1$.
Hence $\Lambda_{k+1}\le1$; $\Lambda_{k+1}(x,x)=0$;
$\Lambda_{k+1}(x,y)\le0$ when $x\in Z_0$ or $y\in Z_1$;
$\Lambda_{k+1}(t_0,t_1)=-1$; and $\Lambda^\times_{k+1}(x,y)=0$ when
$x\in Z_0$, $\Lambda^\times_{k+1}(x,y)\le1$ when $y\in Z_1$,
$\Lambda^\times_{k+1}(x,x)=1$.

\emph{Composites at two vertices of $C$.} By $(\beta)$ and the pointwise
fact just used,
$\Lambda_{k+1}(x,y)\le\max_i\Lambda_k(x^{(i)},y)
\le\max_i\min_j\Lambda_k(x^{(i)},y^{(j)})$ and
$\Lambda_{k+1}(x,y)\le\min_j\Lambda_{k+1}(x,y^{(j)})
\le\min_j\max_i\Lambda_k(x^{(i)},y^{(j)})$; identically in the
multiplicative register.

Every clause of \Cref{def:slack} and of \Cref{def:ratio} is now
accounted for.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The certificate]\label{thm:bc-cert}
With $a,b,c$ as in \Cref{prop:bc-values} put $\ell:=\gamma/c$,
$M_k:=\tfrac14a^{k}$,
\[
  \Xi_k:=\bigl\{\xi\in\mathbb R^{3}:\ \ell\le\xi_1,\xi_2\le M_k,\
  \xi_1\ge a\xi_2,\ \xi_2\ge a\xi_1,\ \xi_0=c\xi_1-\gamma\bigr\},\qquad
  P_k:=\{x\mapsto w^\ast(x)+\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle:\xi\in\Xi_k\},
\]
and let $K$ be the largest $k$ with $aM_k\ge\ell$. Then $(P_k)_{k\le K}$
satisfies the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:convex-barrier-both}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $h_k(\cdot):=\max_{\Xi_k}\langle\cdot,\xi\rangle$, so
$\Lambda(P_k)(x,y)=w^\ast(x)-w^\ast(y)+h_k(\theta_x-\theta_y)$; on $\Xi_k$,
$\langle e_0,\xi\rangle=c\xi_1-\gamma$. For $k\le K$, $\Xi_k$ is a
hexagon with vertices
$(\ell,\ell),(\ell,\ell/a),(\ell/a,\ell),(M_k,M_k),(M_k,aM_k),(aM_k,M_k)$
in the coordinates $(\xi_1,\xi_2)$, all admissible because $aM_k\ge\ell$
(hence $\ell/a\le M_k$) and $a<1$; and $\Xi_{k+1}\subseteq\Xi_k$ because
$M_{k+1}=aM_k$. We verify $(\alpha)$ and $(\beta)$ of
\Cref{lem:lift-reduction}.

$(\alpha)$. The average rows hold by definition of $\theta$. On $\Xi_k$,
$\xi_1,\xi_2\ge\ell>0$ and $\xi_0=c\xi_1-\gamma\ge0$, so $\xi\ge0$ and,
$\theta$ being nonnegative, $z\ge w^\ast\ge0$. Every $\theta_x$ is a
convex combination of $0$ and the $e_j$, and
$\langle e_0,\xi\rangle\le cM_k\le M_k$, so
$\langle\theta_x,\xi\rangle\le M_k\le\tfrac14$; the largest value carried
by a vertex with $\theta_x\ne0$ is $\tfrac34$ (at $\alpha^{i}_e$ and
$\beta^{i}_{2e}$), so $z\le1$; at $g$ and $t_1$, $\theta=0$ and $z=1$; at
$t_0$, $z=0$: these are the box rows and the $Z$ pins. Max rows: at
$v_1$, $z(v_1)\ge z(A^{1}_1)$ reads $\xi_1\ge a\xi_2$ and
$z(v_1)\ge z(B^{1}_1)$ reads $\xi_1\ge b\xi_1$, true because $\xi_1\ge0$
and $b\le1$; at $v_2$ symmetrically; at $v_0$, $z(v_0)\ge z(P)$ reads
$\xi_0\ge0$ and $z(v_0)\ge z(Q)$ reads $\xi_0\ge c\xi_1-\gamma$, an
equality.

$(\beta)$ at $v_0$. On every $P_k$ one has
$z(v_0)=\tfrac12+\xi_0=\tfrac12-\gamma+c\xi_1=z(Q)$ \emph{identically},
so $\Lambda(P_{k+1})(v_0,y)=\Lambda(P_{k+1})(Q,y)\le\Lambda(P_k)(Q,y)$
and $\Lambda_\times(P_{k+1})(v_0,y)=\Lambda_\times(P_{k+1})(Q,y)
\le\Lambda_\times(P_k)(Q,y)$, and $Q$ is one of the two successors.

$(\beta)$ at $v_1$ (the case of $v_2$ exchanges $e_1$ and $e_2$). Since
$w^\ast(A^{1}_1)=w^\ast(B^{1}_1)=w^\ast(v_1)=\tfrac12$, the additive
condition is
\[
  \max\bigl(h_k(a\,e_2-\theta_y),\ h_k(b\,e_1-\theta_y)\bigr)\ \ge\
  h_{k+1}(e_1-\theta_y)\qquad\text{for every }y,
\]
and by \Cref{prop:bc-values} there are four cases.
\emph{$\theta_y=0$}: the left side is at least $bM_k$, the right side is
$M_{k+1}=aM_k$, and $b>a$.
\emph{$\theta_y=c'e_1$}: the right side is $(1-c')M_{k+1}=a(1-c')M_k$,
and at $\xi=(aM_k,M_k)\in\Xi_k$ the first term is
$aM_k-c'aM_k=a(1-c')M_k$.
\emph{$\theta_y=c'e_2$}: as $\xi_2\ge a\xi_1$, the right side is at most
$(1-c'a)M_{k+1}=a(1-c'a)M_k$; at $\xi=(M_k,aM_k)\in\Xi_k$ the second term
is $(b-c'a)M_k$; and
$(b-c'a)-a(1-c'a)=(b-a)-c'a(1-a)=\tfrac32u(1-u)-\tfrac32u\,c'a
=\tfrac32u\,(1-u-c'a)\ge0$ because $c'a\le a=1-\tfrac32u\le1-u$.
\emph{$\theta_y=e_0$}: the right side is $\gamma+(1-c)M_{k+1}$, and at
$\xi=(aM_k,M_k)$ the first term is $aM_k-caM_k+\gamma=\gamma+(1-c)M_{k+1}$.

The multiplicative condition at $v_1$ is
\[
  \max\Bigl(\max_{\Xi_k}\frac{\tfrac12+a\xi_2}{z_\xi(y)},\
  \max_{\Xi_k}\frac{\tfrac12+b\xi_1}{z_\xi(y)}\Bigr)\ \ge\
  \max_{\Xi_{k+1}}\frac{\tfrac12+\xi_1}{z_\xi(y)},\qquad
  z_\xi(y)=w^\ast(y)+\langle\theta_y,\xi\rangle .
\]
The denominator vanishes only at $y=t_0$, where both sides are $+\infty$.
Elsewhere all three maxima are attained at vertices of the hexagons, a
linear-fractional function with positive denominator being both
quasi-convex and quasi-concave.
\emph{$\theta_y=0$}: the denominator is constant and the claim is
$bM_k\ge aM_k$.
\emph{$\theta_y=c'e_1$}: both sides depend only on $\xi_1$, through
$t\mapsto(\tfrac12+t)/(w^\ast(y)+c't)$, which is nondecreasing because
$w^\ast(y)\ge c'/2$ (\Cref{prop:bc-values}); so the right side is
$(\tfrac12+M_{k+1})/(w^\ast(y)+c'M_{k+1})$, and the first term at
$\xi=(aM_k,M_k)\in\Xi_k$ is the same number.
\emph{$\theta_y=c'e_2$}: the right-hand function increases in $\xi_1$ and
decreases in $\xi_2$, so its maximum lies on the edge $\xi_2=a\xi_1$ of
$\Xi_{k+1}$, where it is $g(t)=(\tfrac12+t)/(w^\ast(y)+c'at)$,
$t\in[\ell/a,M_{k+1}]$; $g$ is nondecreasing because
$w^\ast(y)\ge\tfrac14+\tfrac{c'}4\ge\tfrac{c'}2\ge\tfrac{c'a}2$, so the
maximum is $g(M_{k+1})=(\tfrac12+aM_k)/(w^\ast(y)+c'a^{2}M_k)$. The
second term at $\xi=(M_k,aM_k)\in\Xi_k$ is
$(\tfrac12+bM_k)/(w^\ast(y)+c'aM_k)$; writing $W:=w^\ast(y)$, $M:=M_k$,
the required inequality
$(\tfrac12+bM)(W+c'a^{2}M)\ge(\tfrac12+aM)(W+c'aM)$ expands, after
dividing by $\tfrac32u$, to $(1-u)W\ge c'a(\tfrac12+uaM)$. Since
$M\le\tfrac14$ and $a\le1$ the right side is at most
$c'(1-\tfrac32u)(\tfrac12+\tfrac u4)=c'(\tfrac12-\tfrac u2-\tfrac38u^{2})$,
while $W\ge\tfrac14+\tfrac{c'}4$ makes the left side at least
$\tfrac14(1+c')(1-u)$, and
\[
  \tfrac14(1+c')(1-u)-c'\bigl(\tfrac12-\tfrac u2-\tfrac38u^{2}\bigr)
  \;=\;\tfrac{(1-c')(1-u)}4+\tfrac{3c'u^{2}}8\;\ge\;0 .
\]
\emph{$\theta_y=e_0$}: $z_\xi(v_0)=\tfrac12-\gamma+c\xi_1$ and
$t\mapsto(\tfrac12+t)/(\tfrac12-\gamma+ct)$ increases because
$\tfrac12-\gamma\ge\tfrac c2=\tfrac14-2\gamma$; the right side is
attained at $\xi_1=M_{k+1}=aM_k$, and equals the first term at
$\xi=(aM_k,M_k)\in\Xi_k$.

This is $(\beta)$; \Cref{lem:lift-reduction} finishes the proof. The bound
$M_0\le\tfrac14$ is used exactly twice: in the box row of $(\alpha)$ and
in the third multiplicative case.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The lower bound]\label{thm:bc-lower}
For every $k\le K$,
\[
  \Lambda(P_k)(v_0,P)=\Lambda(P_k)(Q,P)=cM_k-\gamma>0,\qquad
  \Lambda(P_k)(Q,v_0)=0,
\]
\[
  \Lambda(P_k)(v_1,A^{1}_1)=(1-a^{2})M_k>0,\quad
  \Lambda(P_k)(v_1,B^{1}_1)=(1-b)M_k>0,\quad
  \Lambda(P_k)(A^{1}_1,B^{1}_1)=a(1-b)M_k>0,\quad
  \Lambda(P_k)(B^{1}_1,A^{1}_1)=(b-a^{2})M_k>0,
\]
symmetrically at $v_2$; each of the six strictly positive quantities is
$>1$ in the multiplicative register, and $\Lambda_\times(P_k)(Q,v_0)=1$
exactly. Consequently no run of the slack calculus, its min-plus closure,
the transport step, the hybrid or the ratio calculus --- in any
interleaving, with any seeding of one by another's output, any up-rounding
onto a grid, and the order transfer of \Cref{thm:convex-barrier-both} ---
decides any controlled vertex of $\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$ within $K$ operator
applications, where
\[
  K+1\;=\;\Bigl\lfloor\frac{\ln\bigl(c/4\gamma\bigr)}{\ln(1/a)}\Bigr\rfloor
  \;\ge\;\Bigl\lfloor\frac{(2^{e+1}-3)(s-4)\ln2}{3}\Bigr\rfloor
\]
is the length of the certificate chain; with $s$ fixed and
$e=(N-2s-11)/12$ that is $2^{\Omega(N)}$. The simulation preorder $\preceq$ of \Cref{def:simorder}
itself decides nothing (\Cref{prop:bc-lmc}); whether seeding the calculi
with its verdicts changes the bound is settled here only by measurement:
at the ten sizes of \Cref{rem:bc-measured} every point of every $P_k$
satisfies every cut $z(x)\le z(y)$ with $x\preceq y$, so at those sizes
the seeded composite obeys the same bound (a proof would need the
certificate to respect the preorder's cuts at every size, which is not
argued).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The displayed quantities. $\Lambda(P_k)(v_0,P)=h_k(e_0)=cM_k-\gamma$,
which is positive for $k\le K$ because $M_k\ge aM_k\ge\ell=\gamma/c$ and
$a<1$; $\Lambda(P_k)(Q,P)=-\gamma+h_k(c\,e_1)=cM_k-\gamma$;
$\Lambda(P_k)(Q,v_0)=-\gamma+h_k(c\,e_1-e_0)=-\gamma+\gamma=0$, since
$c\xi_1-\xi_0\equiv\gamma$ on $\Xi_k$;
$\Lambda(P_k)(v_1,A^{1}_1)=h_k(e_1-a\,e_2)=(1-a^{2})M_k$, attained at
$(M_k,aM_k)$; $\Lambda(P_k)(v_1,B^{1}_1)=h_k((1-b)e_1)=(1-b)M_k$;
$\Lambda(P_k)(A^{1}_1,B^{1}_1)=h_k(a\,e_2-b\,e_1)=a(1-b)M_k$, attained at
$(aM_k,M_k)$; and $\Lambda(P_k)(B^{1}_1,A^{1}_1)=h_k(b\,e_1-a\,e_2)
=(b-a^{2})M_k$, attained at $(M_k,aM_k)$ and positive because
$b-a^{2}=3u(1-\tfrac54u)>0$. Each of these being $>0$, \Cref{lem:ratio-sign}
makes the corresponding multiplicative quantity $>1$; and
$\Lambda_\times(P_k)(Q,v_0)=1$ because $z(Q)=z(v_0)>0$ identically on
$P_k$.

Now let any such run report after $k\le K$ applications from a sound
state (\Cref{thm:convex-barrier-both}). At $v_0$: clause~(i) needs $\Delta(v_0,P)\le0$ or
$\Delta(v_0,Q)\le0$, but $\Delta(v_0,P)\ge\Lambda(P_k)(v_0,P)>0$ by
\Cref{thm:convex-barrier-both} and $\Delta(v_0,Q)\ge w^\ast(v_0)-w^\ast(Q)=\gamma>0$
by soundness; clause~(ii) needs $\Delta(Q,v_0)<0$ or $\Delta(P,v_0)<0$,
but $\Delta(Q,v_0)\ge\Lambda(P_k)(Q,v_0)=0$ and
$\Delta(P,v_0)\ge w^\ast(P)-w^\ast(v_0)=0$; the pair tests need
$\Delta(Q,P)\le0$ or $\Delta(P,Q)\le0$, but
$\Delta(Q,P)\ge\Lambda(P_k)(Q,P)>0$ and $\Delta(P,Q)\ge\gamma>0$. At
$v_1$ and $v_2$ all three of $w^\ast(v_i)$, $w^\ast(A^{i}_1)$,
$w^\ast(B^{i}_1)$ equal $\tfrac12$, so clause~(ii) is blocked by soundness
alone ($\Delta(A^{i}_1,v_i),\Delta(B^{i}_1,v_i)\ge0$), while clause~(i)
and \emph{both} pair tests --- which soundness does not block there, all
three values being equal --- are blocked by the four positive quantities
displayed. The multiplicative register is blocked entry by entry by
\Cref{lem:ratio-sign}, by $\Lambda_\times(P_k)(Q,v_0)=1$, and by
$w^\ast(v_0)/w^\ast(Q)=\tfrac12/(\tfrac12-\gamma)>1$.

The count. $K$ is the largest $k$ with $a\cdot\tfrac14a^{k}\ge\gamma/c$,
i.e.\ $a^{k+1}\ge4\gamma/c$, i.e.\ $k+1\le\ln(c/4\gamma)/\ln(1/a)$; and
$\ln(1/a)=-\ln(1-\tfrac32u)\le\tfrac{3u/2}{1-3u/2}=\tfrac{3}{2^{e+1}-3}$
while $c/4\gamma=2^{s-3}-1\ge2^{s-4}$ for $s\ge5$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Measured, and what the family separates]\label{rem:bc-measured}
Everything above was verified by the route in exact rational arithmetic,
from the game, at $(e,s)=(2,5),(3,5),(4,5),(5,5),(6,5),(2,6),(3,6),(2,7),(3,8),(4,8)$,
$N=45,\dots,93$: the closed forms of $w^\ast$ and $\theta$; stopping;
$w^\ast$ by enumeration of the eight profiles; $Z_0=\{t_0\}$,
$Z_1=\{g,t_1\}$; the two frame identities of \Cref{prop:bc-lmc}; the
greatest fixed point of \Cref{def:simorder} computed outright, transitive,
and relating none of the twelve pairs the four tests read at
$v_0,v_1,v_2$; and at every $k\le K$, $P_k\subseteq Q(G;L_Z,U_Z)$, every
clause of \Cref{def:slack} at $\Lambda(P_k)$ at least $\Lambda(P_{k+1})$
and every clause of \Cref{def:ratio} at $\Lambda_\times(P_k)$ at least
$\Lambda_\times(P_{k+1})$ at all $N^{2}$ entries, every point of $P_k$
obeying every $\preceq$-cut, and none of the twelve tests firing. The
round counts $K+1$ are $2,5,11,22,46$ at $s=5$, $e=2,\dots,6$, doubling
with $e$, and $4,9$ at $s=6$, $5$ at $s=7$, $16,34$ at $s=8$, the closed
form agreeing at every size; the certificate check was also run at
$N=105$, $99$, $79$ ($93$, $144$, $49$ rounds). Both audits rebuilt the
family from the definition and reproduced all of it. Here, with the
harness's own implementations of the calculi seeded with $Z_0,Z_1$ and
both clauses of \Cref{rem:own-successor} at every controlled vertex
(\texttt{scripts/round17-verify/bc\_light.py}, \texttt{bc\_m1.py}, on the
game files \texttt{BC\_\{2,3,4\}\_5.json} archived there, with the harness
of \texttt{scripts/harness/}): on
$\mathrm{BC}(2,5)$ the slack calculus first fires at $v_0$ at round $39$
and its closure at round $21$, both by clause (ii), the ratio calculus
never within $40$ rounds; on $\mathrm{BC}(3,5)$ and $\mathrm{BC}(4,5)$
all three are silent for $40$ rounds; and the preorder relates none of
the twelve pairs at any of the three sizes --- the certificate's
$K+1=2,5,11$ is a lower bound, and the mechanisms are slower still.
$v_1$ and $v_2$ have two successors of equal value, so the stall there is
vacuous in isolation (\Cref{prop:fv-stall}); the content is the stall at
$v_0$, whose successors differ by $\gamma$, and blocking $v_1,v_2$ is what
``decides no controlled vertex'' needs.
Two remarks on the design. The two engine halves carry \emph{different}
straddle amplitudes on purpose: with $\eta_1=\eta_2$ the (match) clause
gives $v_1\preceq v_2\preceq v_1$, and those cuts collapse $\Xi_k$ to its
diagonal, so the seeded composite would escape. And the family answers
the M1 half of \Cref{rem:wedge}'s open item only: by
\Cref{thm:seed-dichotomy} the M3 half is a superpolynomial all-switches
family, which this document does not have, and on $\mathrm{BC}(e,s)$
every $\sigma$ with $\sigma(v_0)=P$ has an empty strictly switchable set,
so all-switches halts within one round from every start and the seeded
calculus $M(1,0)$ decides everything at once: what is exponentially
separated here is the propagation side from one linear solve.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The own-successor rule, and a wedge that defeats it}\label{sec:wedge}

\Cref{rem:own-successor} says a decision rule should compare a controlled vertex
with its own successor, and \Cref{rem:hybrid-lower-not-a-refutation} says a
refutation of the hybrid must drive \emph{those} entries to $0$ rather than the
entries between the two successors. This subsection carries that out. First the
entries in question are identified exactly, which turns the demand into a
geometric one; then a family is exhibited that meets it.

\begin{lemma}[The own-successor entries are support values]\label{lem:wedge-face}
Let $G$ be stopping, let $R_k:=K(\Delta'_{k-1})$ be the polytope round $k$
optimises over, and for $v\in C$ and $i\in\{0,1\}$ let
\[
  g_{v,i}(z):=\begin{cases} z(v)-z(v^{(i)}) & v\in\Vmax,\\
                            z(v^{(i)})-z(v) & v\in\Vmin,\end{cases}
\]
the controlled rows of \Cref{def:transport} read as nonnegative functionals.
Then for $k\ge1$
\[
  \Delta_k\bigl(v,v^{(i)}\bigr)=\max_{R_k}g_{v,i}\ \ge 0,
  \qquad
  \Delta_k\bigl(v^{(i)},v\bigr)=-\min_{R_k}g_{v,i}\ \le 0
\]
at $v\in\Vmax$, with the two exchanged at $v\in\Vmin$. Hence the own-successor
test fires at $v$ at round $k$ if and only if, for some $i$, either
$g_{v,i}\equiv0$ on $R_k$ or $\min_{R_k}g_{v,i}>0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$\Delta_k=\Lambda(R_k)$ by \Cref{lem:hybrid-cutting}, and
$\Lambda(R_k)(x,y)=\max_{R_k}(z(x)-z(y))$ by definition; the two displays are
that identity at $(v,v^{(i)})$ and at $(v^{(i)},v)$. The signs are the
controlled rows of $Q(G)$, which every $z\in R_k\subseteq Q(G)$ satisfies.
Clause (i) of \Cref{rem:own-successor} asks $\Delta_k(v,v^{(i)})\le0$, i.e.\
$\max_{R_k}g_{v,i}\le0$, which with $g_{v,i}\ge0$ means $g_{v,i}\equiv0$;
clause (ii) asks $\Delta_k(v^{(i)},v)<0$, i.e.\ $\min_{R_k}g_{v,i}>0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[What a certificate must do]\label{cor:wedge-cert}
Let $(P_k)$ be a chain as in \Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier}. If for every
$k\le K$, every $v\in C$ and both $i$,
\[
  \min_{P_k}g_{v,i}=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\max_{P_k}g_{v,i}>0,
\]
then the own-successor rule fires at no controlled vertex at any round $\le K$.
Equivalently, each of the $2|C|$ hyperplanes $\{g_{v,i}=0\}$ must \emph{support}
$P_k$ and \emph{touch} it without containing it.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
\Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier} gives $\Delta_k\ge\Lambda(P_k)$, so
$\Delta_k(v,v^{(i)})\ge\max_{P_k}g_{v,i}>0$ and
$\Delta_k(v^{(i)},v)\ge-\min_{P_k}g_{v,i}=0$; neither clause of
\Cref{rem:own-successor} fires.
\end{proof}

This is a strictly stronger demand than the one \Cref{thm:hybrid-lower} meets.
There the certificate controls one functional per Max vertex, and only its sign;
here it must control $2|C|$ \emph{prescribed} directions, two-sidedly, with
equality on the min side. The family below meets it by placing the entanglement
on the \emph{non-optimal} branch, which is exactly what $CC$ does not do.

\begin{definition}[The wedge family]\label{def:wedge}
For $e\ge1$, $j\ge2$, $m\ge j+2$ put $\varepsilon=2^{-e}$, $\delta=2^{-j}$,
$\gamma=2^{-m}$. Let $WD(e,j,m)$ have $\Vmax=\{v_1,v_2\}$, $\Vmin=\emptyset$,
every other non-sink average, and
\[
 \begin{aligned}
  &H\to(t_1,t_0);\qquad
   w_1\to(t_0,w_2),\quad w_q\to(t_1,w_{q+1})\ (2\le q<L),\quad w_L\to(t_1,t_0),
   \ \ L=m-j;\\
  &a_{i,q}\to(v_i,a_{i,q+1})\ (q<e),\qquad a_{i,e}\to(v_i,H);\\
  &b_i\to(v_i,c_{i,1}),\qquad
   c_{i,q}\to(v_{3-i},c_{i,q+1})\ (q<j-1),\qquad c_{i,j-1}\to(v_{3-i},w_1);\\
  &v_i\to(a_{i,1},b_i).
 \end{aligned}
\]
Then $N=2e+j+m+5$, the game is stopping and one-player,
$\val(w_1)=\tfrac12-2^{j-m}$, $\val(v_i)=\val(a_{i,1})=\tfrac12$ and
$\val(b_i)=\tfrac12-\gamma$, so both Max vertices are value-distinguishing with
gap $\gamma$ and $a_{i,1}$ is the optimal successor. The bound $m\ge j+2$,
that is $L\ge2$, is needed: at $L=1$ the two prescriptions for $w_1$ ---
$w_1\to(t_0,w_2)$ and $w_L\to(t_1,t_0)$ --- contradict each other, and reading
the second gives $\val(w_1)=\tfrac12$ against the stated
$\tfrac12-2^{j-m}=0$; every use of the family below has $L=4$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[The wedge defeats the own-successor rule]\label{prop:wedge}
On $WD(2j,j,j+4)$, which has $N=6j+9$, the own-successor test of
\Cref{rem:own-successor} --- seeded with the free $Z_0,Z_1$ bounds, read in
\emph{both} directions, at \emph{every} controlled vertex --- first fires at
round
\[
  4,\ 8,\ 17,\ 35,\ 70,\ 140 \qquad\text{for } j=2,\dots,7,\quad
  N=21,\ 27,\ 33,\ 39,\ 45,\ 51,
\]
so the first firing round doubles with every six additional vertices. The weaker
pair test does not fire earlier: on $WD(4,2,6)$ it also first fires at round
$4$, with $\Delta_k(b_1,a_{1,1})=\tfrac7{512},\tfrac5{1024},\tfrac1{2048},
-\tfrac7{512}$.
\end{proposition}

\Cref{prop:wedge} is a measurement, but it need not be: the clause
condition of \Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier} can be verified once and for
all, by the method \Cref{thm:hybrid-lower} uses on $CC(L,m)$.

\begin{definition}[The wedge certificate chain]\label{def:wedge-chain}
For $WD(e,j,m)$ put $u:=2^{1-j}$, $\lambda:=1-u$, $\gamma:=2^{-m}$,
$\mu:=2\gamma/\lambda$ and, for $e>j$,
$\mu^{\dagger}:=\mu/(1-2^{\,j-e})$. In the coordinates
$\xi=z|_C-\val|_C$ of \Cref{rem:wedge} set
\[
  M_0:=\tfrac12,\qquad M_{k+1}:=\lambda M_k-2\gamma,\qquad
  P_k:=\bigl\{\xi\in[0,M_k]^2:\ \xi_1\ge\lambda\xi_2-2\gamma,\
  \xi_2\ge\lambda\xi_1-2\gamma\bigr\},
\]
so that $P_0=Q(WD)-\val$, and let $K:=\max\{k:M_k\ge\mu^{\dagger}\}$ and
$\delta_k:=M_k-M_{k+1}=uM_k+2\gamma$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Vertices]\label{lem:wedge-verts}
If $M_k\ge\mu^{\dagger}$ then $M_k>\mu>0$, $0<M_{k+1}\le M_k$,
$\delta_k\ge\mu$, and $P_k$ is the convex hull of
$A_0=(0,0)$, $A_1=(\mu,0)$, $A_2=(M_k,M_{k+1})$, $A_3=(M_k,M_k)$,
$A_4=(M_{k+1},M_k)$, $A_5=(0,\mu)$. Moreover $P_{k+1}\subseteq P_k$ and,
for $c$ with $c_1\ge0\ge c_2$,
\[
  h_{P_k}(c):=\max_{\xi\in P_k}\langle c,\xi\rangle
  =\max\bigl(c_1\mu,\ c_1M_k+c_2M_{k+1}\bigr).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$\mu^{\dagger}>\mu$ as $0<2^{j-e}<1$; $M_{k+1}>0$ is $M_k>\mu$;
$M_{k+1}\le M_k$ is $\lambda<1$; and
$\delta_k=uM_k+2\gamma\ge u\mu+2\gamma=2\gamma(u+\lambda)/\lambda=\mu$. The
six points satisfy the four inequalities, $A_1$ and $A_5$ because
$\lambda\mu-2\gamma=0$ and $A_2$, $A_4$ because one wedge row is tight and
the other slack. Conversely $P_k$ is cut out by six lines, of whose
fifteen pairwise intersections the parallel pair gives nothing and the
remaining thirteen give exactly $A_0,\dots,A_5$ together with seven
infeasible points --- $\{\xi_1=0,\xi_2=M_k\}$ and its mirror need
$0\ge M_{k+1}$; $\xi_1=0$ with the second wedge line gives
$\xi_2=-2\gamma$ and its mirror likewise; $\xi_1=M_k$ with the first gives
$\xi_2=(M_k+2\gamma)/\lambda>M_k$ and its mirror likewise; and the two
wedge lines meet where $\xi_1(1-\lambda^2)=-2\gamma(1+\lambda)<0$.
For the support function, with $c_1\ge0\ge c_2$ the values at
$A_0,A_3,A_4,A_5$ are dominated by those at $A_1,A_2$, since
$(c_1+c_2)M_k\le c_1M_k+c_2M_{k+1}$, $(c_1-c_2)(M_{k+1}-M_k)\le0$,
$c_2\mu\le0$ and $0\le c_1\mu$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The wedge, proved]\label{thm:wedge-proved}
Let $j\ge2$, $e\ge2j-1$ and $m\ge j+2$. Then for every $k\le K$ the
hybrid of \Cref{def:hybrid} with the free $Z$-seed satisfies
$\Delta_k\ge\Lambda(P_k)$ on $WD(e,j,m)$, and the own-successor test of
\Cref{rem:own-successor}, read in both directions, fires at neither Max
vertex at any round $k\le K$. The same silence holds for
\Cref{def:slack}, \Cref{def:trans-slack} and \Cref{def:transport}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier} it suffices that every clause of
\Cref{def:slack} evaluated at $\Lambda(P_k)$ is at least
$\Lambda(P_{k+1})$. Most are free. Each $z\in P_k$ satisfies the rows of
$Q(G)$, so at $y\in\Vmax$ we have $z(y)\ge z(y^{(j)})$ and hence
$\Lambda(P_k)(x,y^{(j)})\ge\Lambda(P_{k+1})(x,y)$ for both $j$; dually the
(up) clause at $x\in\Vmin$; and at an average vertex $z$ is the exact mean
of its successors, so
$z(x)-z(y)=\tfrac12\sum_j(z(x)-z(y^{(j)}))$, which covers the average
(up), (down) and both average composites. The $Z$ clauses hold because the
free seed pins $z$ on $Z_0$ and $Z_1$, the constant and diagonal clauses
trivially. As $\Vmin=\emptyset$ only the (up) clause at $v_1,v_2$ and the
$\Vmax\times\Vmax$ composites remain.

For the (up) clause take $i=1$; $i=2$ is the automorphism exchanging the
two indices. A maximum of two maxima over the same set being the maximum
of the pointwise maximum,
\[
  \max\bigl(\Lambda(P_k)(a_{1,1},y),\Lambda(P_k)(b_1,y)\bigr)
  =\max_{\xi\in P_k}\bigl[z(v_1)-D(\xi)-z(y)\bigr],\qquad
  D(\xi):=\min\Bigl(2^{-e}\xi_1,\ \gamma+\tfrac12(\xi_1-\lambda\xi_2)\Bigr),
\]
$D\ge0$ on $Q(G)$ by \Cref{rem:wedge}. Both sides carry the constant
$\val(v_1)-\val(y)$, so with $c:=\epsilon_1-\theta_y$ the claim is
$\Phi_k(c):=\max_{P_k}[\langle c,\xi\rangle-D(\xi)]\ge h_{P_{k+1}}(c)$.
The vectors $\theta_y$ are $0$, $\epsilon_i$, $\alpha\epsilon_i$ with
$\alpha\in\{1-2^{-r}:1\le r\le e\}$, and
$\tfrac12(\epsilon_i+\lambda\epsilon_o)$, so every such $c$ has
$c_1\ge0\ge c_2$ and lies in
\[
  \mathcal C=\{(\beta,0)\}\cup\{(1,-\alpha)\}\cup
  \bigl\{(1-\tfrac\lambda2,-\tfrac12),(\tfrac12,-\tfrac\lambda2)\bigr\}.
\]
Evaluating at $A_0$, $A_4$ and $A_2$, where $D(A_0)=0$,
$D(A_4)=\min(\cdot,\gamma+\tfrac12(M_{k+1}-\lambda M_k))=0$ and
$D(A_2)\le2^{-e}M_k$, gives
$\Phi_k(c)\ge\max\bigl(0,\ c_1M_{k+1}+c_2M_k,\ c_1M_k+c_2M_{k+1}-2^{-e}M_k\bigr)$.
If $c_2=0$ then $h_{P_{k+1}}(c)=\beta M_{k+1}$, attained at $A_4$. If
$c_2<0$, two bounds are needed. For
$\Phi_k(c)\ge c_1M_{k+1}+c_2M_{k+2}$, the point $A_2$ and
$M_{k+1}-M_{k+2}=\lambda\delta_k$ give a difference
$(c_1+\lambda c_2)\delta_k-2^{-e}M_k$, and $\delta_k\ge uM_k$ reduces it to
$u(c_1+\lambda c_2)\ge2^{-e}$; now $c_1+\lambda c_2$ is $1-\lambda\alpha\ge u$
at $(1,-\alpha)$, is $u$ at $(1-\tfrac\lambda2,-\tfrac12)$ and is
$\tfrac12u(1+\lambda)\ge\tfrac u2$ at $(\tfrac12,-\tfrac\lambda2)$, so
$u(c_1+\lambda c_2)\ge u^2/2=2^{1-2j}\ge2^{-e}$ by $e\ge2j-1$. For
$\Phi_k(c)\ge c_1\mu$, again at $A_2$ and using $\mu-2\gamma=u\mu$: at
$(1,-\alpha)$ with $\alpha\le1$ the difference is at least
$(u-2^{-e})M_k-u\mu\ge0$ whenever $M_k\ge\mu/(1-2^{\,j-1-e})$, which
follows from $M_k\ge\mu^{\dagger}$; at $(1-\tfrac\lambda2,-\tfrac12)$ the
same expression appears; and at $(\tfrac12,-\tfrac\lambda2)$ the difference
is $(\tfrac12u(1+\lambda)-2^{-e})M_k-\tfrac12u(1+\lambda)\mu$, nonnegative
once $M_k\ge\mu/(1-2^{1-e}/(u(1+\lambda)))$, and
$2^{1-e}/(u(1+\lambda))\le2^{\,j-e}$.

The $\Vmax\times\Vmax$ composites reduce to the same estimate through
$\max_i\min_j\le\min_j\max_i$ and the branch $a_{1,1}$, and need only
$e\ge2j-2$; this case was checked numerically as well, on all $60$ triples
listed below at $k\le15$. Silence is then \Cref{cor:wedge-cert}: on $P_k$ one has
$\Lambda(P_k)(v_1,a_{1,1})=2^{-e}M_k>0$ and
$\Lambda(P_k)(v_1,b_1)\ge\gamma+\mu/2>0$, while
$\Lambda(P_k)(a_{1,1},v_1)=0$ at $A_0$ and $\Lambda(P_k)(b_1,v_1)=0$ at
$A_4$, so no entry the test reads is decisive. The transfer to
\Cref{def:slack}, \Cref{def:trans-slack} and \Cref{def:transport} is that
each is dominated by the hybrid entrywise.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The count]\label{cor:wedge-count}
For $e=2j$ and $m=j+4$, so $N=6j+9$, one has
$M_k=\lambda^k\tfrac9{16}-\tfrac1{16}$ and
\[
  K=1,\,6,\,15,\,33,\,68,\,138,\,279,\,560\qquad(j=2,\dots,9),
\]
with $K\ge2^{\,j-1}$ for $j\ge3$ and hence $K\ge2^{(N-15)/6}$. The
silence of \Cref{thm:wedge-proved} is therefore $2^{\Omega(N)}$ rounds,
unconditionally.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The closed form is the linear recursion with
$2\gamma/(1-\lambda)=2^{\,j-m}=\tfrac1{16}$. For $j\ge3$ put
$n:=2^{\,j-1}\ge4$; since $(1-1/n)^n$ increases in $n$,
$\lambda^n\ge(3/4)^4>0.316$ and $M_n>0.316\cdot\tfrac9{16}-\tfrac1{16}>0.115$,
while $\mu^{\dagger}=2^{-j-3}/(\lambda(1-2^{-j}))$ decreases in $j$ from
$\tfrac1{42}<0.024$ at $j=3$; so $M_{2^{j-1}}>\mu^{\dagger}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:wedge}
The mechanism is the wedge. In the coordinates $\xi=z|_C-\val|_C$ the polytope
$Q(WD)-\val$ is
$\{\xi\in[0,\tfrac12]^2:\lambda\xi_1-2\gamma\le\xi_2\le(\xi_1+2\gamma)/\lambda\}$
with $\lambda=1-2^{1-j}$, a thin wedge with apex at the origin; the two
hyperplanes $\{g_{v_i,\bar i}=0\}$ support it at the apex and touch it there
without containing it, which is exactly \Cref{cor:wedge-cert}, and each round
shrinks the wedge by the factor $\lambda$ only. The chain $P_k$ of \Cref{def:wedge-chain} makes this
unconditional: \Cref{thm:wedge-proved} proves the clause condition of
\Cref{thm:hybrid-convex-barrier} for every $j\ge2$, $e\ge2j-1$,
$m\ge j+2$, and \Cref{cor:wedge-count} turns it into $2^{\Omega(N)}$
silent rounds. \Cref{prop:wedge} remains the sharper statement at the
sizes it covers --- the certificate is two rounds short of the measurement
throughout --- but it is no longer the only one. We verified the proof's
load-bearing inequality, $\Phi_k(c)\ge h_{P_{k+1}}(c)$ at every direction
and every $k\le K$, in exact rational arithmetic over the $60$ triples
$j\le6$, $e\in\{2j-1,2j,2j+1,3j\}$, $m\in\{j+2,j+4,j+6\}$, and the
conclusion $\Delta_k\ge\Lambda(P_k)$ directly against a run of the hybrid
at $j=2,3,4$, with no violation in either.

The scope is narrower than it first appears, and this is the important
qualification. $WD$ is one-player, and from each of its four Max profiles one round of
switch-all improvement reaches $\Opt$, so by \Cref{thm:seed-dichotomy} the
seeded calculus \Cref{def:seeded} as $M(1,0)$ decides both Max vertices at
slack round zero --- $M(0,0)$ does not from the profile $v_i\mapsto b_i$,
where the seed's lower bound is inexact (see the proof of
\Cref{thm:seed-dichotomy}). It
does not defeat \Cref{def:simorder} either, and for a different reason: the
greatest fixed point there contains the pair $(v_i,a_{i,1})$, so
$w^\ast(v_i)\le w^\ast(a_{i,1})$ is derived, which at a Max vertex forces
equality and proves $a_{i,1}$ optimal --- firing direction (i) of
\Cref{rem:own-successor}, at round zero. We verified this on $WD(4,2,6)$ and
$WD(6,3,7)$, the preorder being sound there with no violation among its derived
pairs. So the family defeats \Cref{def:slack}, \Cref{def:trans-slack},
\Cref{def:transport} and the hybrid of \Cref{sec:hybrid}, and neither
\Cref{def:simorder} nor \Cref{sec:seeded}. It defeats the multiplicative mechanisms as well: on
$WD(2j,j,j+4)$ the min-times closure of \Cref{def:ratio} first fires at
rounds $30$ and $87$ for $j=2,3$ and not within $170$ rounds for $j=4$
(the upward-rounded variant of \Cref{prop:ratio-closure}, whose rounding
can only delay a firing, so the $170$ is not a statement about the exact
closure) --- $N=21,27,33$ --- against $21$, $51$ and $112$ for
\Cref{def:trans-slack}, so it is defeated by the same family and is on
this one the slower of the two, having been the faster on $H_m$
(\Cref{prop:ratio-closure}). Among the mechanisms that \emph{propagate}
a matrix, the wedge is therefore the one obstruction here that none
evades; the two mechanisms it fails against, \Cref{def:simorder} and
\Cref{sec:seeded}, both decide it without iterating at all.

One further attempt is worth recording because it looks like the right
one and is not. In the coordinates $\xi$ a multiplicative cut
$z(p)\le R(p,q)z(q)$ reads
$\xi(p)\le R\xi(q)+(R\,w^\ast(q)-w^\ast(p))$, whose constant vanishes
when $R$ is exact; an exact ratio cut is therefore a \emph{cone through
the apex}, which is the shape the wedge is made of. Adjoining all such
cuts, with $R$ taken from \Cref{def:ratio} and its min-times closure, to
the difference cuts of \Cref{def:hybrid} changes nothing: on
$WD(2j,j,j+4)$ for $j=2,3,4$ the first firing round remains $4,8,17$,
and the polygon is \emph{identical} at every round --- on $WD(4,2,6)$
the augmented programme adds $346$ to $348$ ratio cuts per round beside
the $218$ to $372$ difference cuts and the vertex set never moves. The
reason is that a ratio read off the polytope is by construction a
supporting half-plane of it, and on this family the ratios the clauses
supply are never tighter than the ones the polytope already implies. The
obstruction is the rate at which the cone narrows, $\lambda=1-2^{1-j}$
per round, and adding further cones through the same apex does not
change a rate. What would be needed to defeat that as well is a two-player
wedge, and \Cref{thm:seed-dichotomy} says any such instance is a stopping game
on which switch-all improvement has not converged --- the same pivot that
\Cref{cor:f-auso} and \Cref{prop:auso-size} leave open.
\end{remark}


\section{Summary}\label{sec:summary}

\paragraph{Foundations, proved from first principles.} The monotone
fixed-point theory of the Shapley operator (\Cref{sec:operators}); for
stopping games, a strategy-independent absorption estimate with exponent
$|\Vavg|$ (\Cref{lem:absorption}), the resulting contraction and
uniqueness of fixed points (\Cref{thm:contraction}), and determinacy
together with the identification of those fixed points with the game
values (\Cref{thm:stopping-determinacy}) --- so that outside
\Cref{thm:determinacy} and the three imports named in the abstract
nothing classical is imported. A denominator bound
$8^{r/2}$ depending only on the number $r$ of average vertices
(\Cref{lem:denominator}), sharpened to $2^{r}$ and shown attained
(\Cref{lem:denominator-sharp}), and an explicit reduction to the stopping
case with every constant computed (\Cref{thm:stopping-transform}).
Without any stopping hypothesis, $\val$ and every $\val_\sigma$ are the
\emph{least} fixed points of their operators (\Cref{thm:lfp-general}),
proved by a first-passage decomposition rather than by exchanging a limit
with an infimum.

\paragraph{Positive algorithmic results.} Several tractable regimes, each
with a complete proof, most of whose algorithms we implemented and checked
against exhaustive computation, as recorded at the theorems:
arbitrary SSGs with $a=O(\log N)$ average vertices, in time
$\mathrm{poly}(N)2^{a}$ and with no stopping hypothesis
(\Cref{thm:few-avg}), the exponent being improvable to the escape
exponent $d(G)\le a$, which \Cref{prop:fk-family} separates from $a$ by
$\Theta(N)$ (\Cref{thm:few-escape}); games whose cycles are \emph{pure}, that is in which
no cycle passes through an average vertex (\Cref{thm:avg-acyclic}) or,
dually, none passes through a controlled vertex
(\Cref{thm:player-free}), both in polynomial time; one-player games, by
an explicit linear program (\Cref{thm:one-player}); all three being
instances of one theorem that is strictly stronger than the three
together, namely that it suffices for \emph{some single colour} to be
absent from every cycle (\Cref{thm:kacyclic,prop:kacyclic-strict});
games in which no two Max vertices are mutually reachable, and more
generally in which the components of the Max-reachability digraph have
size $O(\log N)$ (\Cref{thm:bounded-components}), a class that
\Cref{prop:k1-family} shows meets none of the four combinatorial classes
preceding it (its witness does carry an escape certificate); a quantitative
class defined by a linear-programming certificate for the rate at which
two-sided value iteration contracts (\Cref{thm:escape-class}), with a
family inside it and outside all four combinatorial ones
(\Cref{prop:escape-family}); the stopping games whose value alphabet has
a polynomially bounded common denominator, solved in $O(N^{2}D^{2})$ with
no advance knowledge of $D$ (\Cref{thm:few-denominator}) --- a class read
off the answer and not the presentation, which \Cref{prop:fv-family}
shows lies inside none of the five; the games with a bounded
modulator number, solved in $N^{O(\mu)}$ by a fixed-point search over a
frozen set of $\mu$ vertices, with $M_n$ a family inside that class and
outside five of the six others (\Cref{thm:modulator,prop:modulator-family}), and the
stopping games of bounded treewidth, solved in quasipolynomial time
(\Cref{thm:qp}); and, in general, a
Las~Vegas algorithm of expected time $e^{2\sqrt n}\,\mathrm{poly}(N)$
obtained from a full, tie-robust analysis of Ludwig's random-facet
strategy improvement, the specialisation of the Kalai and
Matou\v{s}ek--Sharir--Welzl scheme
(\Cref{thm:rf-correct,thm:rf-bound,lem:fbound,thm:subexp}). The last is
subexponential, not polynomial, and is the strongest general upper bound
here.

\paragraph{Where the difficulty is.} The optimal Max strategies of a
stopping game form exactly the subcube of greedy strategies
(\Cref{thm:opt-subcube}), and from every strategy at most $|\Vmax|$
single strictly improving switches reach it (\Cref{thm:short-path}). So
no lower bound can come from the length of improving paths
(\Cref{cor:selection}); everything is in the \emph{selection} of
switches. That selection problem is exactly the original problem:
\textsc{SSG-Value} is polynomial-time equivalent to comparing two vertex
values, $n$ non-adaptive comparisons determine an optimal strategy
(\Cref{thm:compare-equivalence}), and deciding whether one given vertex
is badly chosen is again equivalent to the whole problem
(\Cref{cor:wrong-equivalence}), as is naming the average vertex of
largest value (\Cref{thm:top}). Seen from outside, a game plugged into a
larger one is a controlled vertex with two chance actions, one for each
player's value, and the visits of an optimal pair are tied to the value
gaps at the average vertices by an exact energy identity, which bounds
what any composition can amplify (\Cref{sec:composition}); the two
qualitative sets are computed inside the model (\Cref{prop:qualitative}). The information content is smaller than the
first count suggests --- the order of the average values already determines
everything (\Cref{thm:order-determines}) --- but not the difficulty, since
each comparison is again an instance of the problem.

\paragraph{Refutations and barriers, each with an explicit instance.}
All-switches does not dominate partial switches
(\Cref{thm:all-switches-refuted}) and can take more than $|\Vmax|$
iterations while undoing its own choices
(\Cref{prop:allswitch-overshoot}), while the least-index and
smallest-gap rules take $2^{(N-2)/2}-1$ iterations on an acyclic
one-player ladder that back-substitution solves in linear time
(\Cref{thm:ladder}); the induced orientation of the
combined two-player cube can be cyclic, so no potential lives on it
(\Cref{thm:cyclic-uso}); value iteration needs $2^{\Omega(N)}$ steps
already on games with no players at all (\Cref{thm:vi-lower}); there is
no naive Hamming potential (\Cref{thm:hamming-refuted}) and seven natural
polynomial-time selection rules each fail (\Cref{prop:rules-fail}), while
the wrong vertices can be a $1/|\Vmax|$ fraction of the switchable ones
(\Cref{prop:needle}); the exact gain of a switch is a bounded transfer
impedance divided by an escape probability that can be $2^{-\Theta(N)}$,
so no potential of polynomial range can work (\Cref{thm:impedance});
each $\val_\sigma$ lies on a grid of mesh at least $2^{-a}$, which caps every single-switch
improving rule at $N2^{a}$ switches, every multi-switch one at $N4^{a}$,
and is essentially attained by the ladder
(\Cref{thm:switch-count}), so counting arguments of that shape are
settled in both directions; and
no parameter blind to which player owns which vertex can serve as a
hardness certificate, since it is already large on easy one-player
instances (\Cref{rem:owner-blind}); and no sound rule deciding a single
vertex can read only a bounded-radius neighbourhood of it
(\Cref{prop:locality}). Finally, solving the deterministic part of the
game exactly --- an acceleration that collapses the ladder's exponential
run to a single round (\Cref{prop:residue-ladder}) --- is undone by a
value-preserving normalisation adding at most $4N$ average vertices,
which leaves no redex for a preprocessing step to recognise, so no rule
of that kind runs in polynomially many rounds unless all-switches already
does (\Cref{thm:normalform-barrier}); the level above it, which frees one
average vertex, escapes and is a sound improving rule that is not a
